Summarizing the results of this Subsection, we obtain the following Corollary
3.5
, which extracts the subleading residual terms
\mathcal{E}_{0},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{4}
from the quantum energy.

Corollary 3.5
.

Let
f\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
be reflection anti-symmetric,
\Phi_{\alpha}\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)
the state defined in Eq. (
3.27
), and let
\mathcal{E}_{0},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{4}
be defined in Eq. (
3.14
), Eq. (
3.19
), Eq. (
3.23
), Eq. (
3.30
) and Eq. (
3.31
), as well as
\mu_{\alpha}
below Eq. (
3.26
). Then

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha,p},\mathbb{H}_{p}\Psi_{\alpha,p}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}=E_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\mu_{\alpha}^{2}\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\!\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\!L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle+|p|^{2}\mathcal{E}_{0}-2\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathfrak{Re}\!\left[\mathcal{E}_{j}\right].

Note that we have removed the projection onto the real part
\mathfrak{Re}
in the essential contribution

(3.32)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\!\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\!L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)},

since
\Phi_{\alpha}\geq 0
, see Lemma
3.1
, and
v^{\Lambda}
is real-valued, and therefore the term in Eq. (
3.32
) is an element of
\mathbb{R}
. Furthermore, it is notable that the expression in Eq. (
3.32
) is an expectation value with respect to the state
\Phi_{\alpha}
, which is a low energy state of
\mathbb{H}^{K}
having a localized median, and therefore expected to satisfy Bose-Einstein condensation, see Lemma
9.2
. Making use of Bose-Einstein condensation, we are going to evaluate the expression in Eq. (
3.32
) in the following Section
4
.

4.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In order to verify the main Theorem
1.1
, we will make use of the results on the regularized median
m_{q}
obtained in Section
5
and the fact that
\Phi_{\alpha}
satisfies Bose-Einstein condensation, which we are going to verify in Section
9
, in order to evaluate the term in Eq. (
3.32
). As a first step, we are going to identify the leading term in the squared increment in Lemma
4.1

\displaystyle\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}.

For this purpose, we are going to use Lemma
5.2
from Section
5
, which tells us that for admissible measures
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}

(4.1)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho)+\int H\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)-m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|g\!*\!(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)\|_{TV}\!\left(\|g\!*\!(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)\|_{TV}+\|g\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}\right),

where
\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}=\rho_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}
is the Pekar measure and the function
H:=H_{q,\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}}^{\chi}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
is defined in components
H=(H_{1},H_{2},H_{3})
according to Eq. (
5.7
) as

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ H_{j}(y):=\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\Bigg(\left[\mathds{1}_{[x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}),x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})]}(y_{j})\,y_{j}\right]*\chi

\displaystyle\!+\!x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\left(\!\frac{1}{2}\!+\!q\!-\!f_{j,x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})}(y)\!\!\right)\!-\!x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\left(\frac{1}{2}\!-\!q\!-\!f_{j,x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})}(y)\!\!\right)\!\!\Bigg).

Note that the bounded function
f_{j,y}
is defined in Eq. (
5.1
), the regularized quantiles
x^{-}_{j,q}
and
x^{+}_{j,q}
are introduced in Eq. (
5.6
) and the total variation between
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
is defined as

\displaystyle\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}:=\sup_{\|f\|_{\infty}=1}\left|\int f\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}-\int f\mathrm{d}\rho\right|.

Furthermore, we have used in Eq. (
4.1
) that
m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)=0
, which follows from the fact that the Pekar measure
\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}
is radial. In the following recall that we have chosen the mollifier
\chi
as a convolution
\chi=g*g
of another mollifier
g
and defined
g_{T}
as a rescaled version of
g
in Subsection
2.4
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Given a function
f_{0}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
T>0
, let us make the choice
f:=g_{T}*f_{0}
in Eq. (
3.5
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
T>0
, and
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
satisfying that
(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}},\rho,\rho^{\prime})
is an admissible triple in the sense of Lemma
5.2

\displaystyle\left|\Big[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho^{\prime})-p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho)\Big]^{2}-\left[\int p\!\cdot\!(H+f)\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int\partial_{y_{j}}(p\!\cdot\!f)\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\!\!\int H_{j}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)\right]^{2}\right|

(4.2)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \leq C\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|^{2}_{TV}\!\left(\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}+\|g\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}+\|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}\right)|p|^{2}.

Proof.

Let us first write
\mathcal{B}(\rho^{\prime})-\mathcal{B}(\rho)
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(\rho^{\prime})-\mathcal{B}(\rho)=m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-m_{q}(\chi*\rho)+\int f(y-m_{q}(\chi*\rho))\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)

(4.3)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\int\big[f(y-m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime}))-f(y-m_{q}(\chi*\rho))\big]\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}.

Furthermore, we introduce for the sake of convenience the Landau notation

\displaystyle O_{*}:=O\!\left(\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}\!\left(\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}+\|g\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}+\|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}\right)\right).

By Eq. (
4.1
), and the fact that taking the convolution with
g
is a contraction of the total variation, i.e.
\|g*(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
, we immediately obtain

(4.4)

\displaystyle m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-m_{q}(\chi*\rho)=\int H\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)+O_{*}.

Since any measure
\nu
in an admissible triple satisfies
\|\nu\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\int\mathrm{d}\nu\leq D
, and
m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)=0
, we furthermore have
\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho)\right|\leq C\|g\!*\!\left(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
for a suitable constant
C>0
. Hence

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left|\int f(y-m_{q}(\chi*\rho))\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)-\int f\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m_{q}(\chi*\rho)\!\cdot\!\nabla f(y-sm_{q}(\chi*\rho))\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)(y)\mathrm{d}s\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|g\!*\!\left(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=O_{*}.

Regarding the final term in Eq. (
4.3
) let us use that for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho)\right|+\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})\right|\leq C\left(\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\|g\!*\!\left(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\right),

as well as Eq. (
4.4
) in order to obtain

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \int\big[f(y-m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime}))-f(y-m_{q}(\chi*\rho))\big]\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int\big[f_{0}(y-m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime}))-f_{0}(y-m_{q}(\chi*\rho))\big]\mathrm{d}(g_{T}*\rho^{\prime})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-m_{q}(\chi*\rho)\right)_{j}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{y_{j}}f_{0}(y-sm_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-(1-s)m_{q}(\chi*\rho))\mathrm{d}(g_{T}*\rho^{\prime})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int H_{j}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{y_{j}}f_{0}(y-sm_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-(1-s)m_{q}(\chi*\rho))\mathrm{d}(g_{T}*\rho^{\prime})+O_{*}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int H_{j}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{y_{j}}f_{0}(y-sm_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-(1-s)m_{q}(\chi*\rho))\mathrm{d}(g_{T}*\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})+O_{*}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int H_{j}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{y_{j}}f_{0}(y)\mathrm{d}(g_{T}*\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})+O_{*}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int H_{j}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{y_{j}}f(y)\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}+O_{*}.

Summarizing what we have so far yields

(4.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(\rho^{\prime})-\mathcal{B}(\rho)=\int(H+f)\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int\partial_{y_{j}}f\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\!\!\int H_{j}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)+O_{*}.

Since the terms
\int(H+f)\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)
and
\int\partial_{y_{j}}f\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\!\!\int H_{j}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)
are bounded by
C\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
, the left hand side of Eq. (
4.2
) is bounded for a suitable constant
C>0
by

\displaystyle\ \ \ C\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}^{2}\!\left(\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}\!+\|g\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}+\!\|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\left(\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}\!+\|g\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}+\!\|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}\!+\!1\right)\!|p|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(4D+1)\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}^{2}\left(\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}\!+\|g\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}+\!\|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{TV}\right)|p|^{2},

where we have used that
\|\nu\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\|g_{T}*\nu\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\|g*\nu\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq D
for
\nu\in\{\rho_{0},\rho,\rho^{\prime}\}
.
∎

In order to successfully apply Lemma
4.1
for the analysis of the term

(4.6)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\!\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\!L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)},

it is imperative to control the expectation value of the total variation

(4.7)

\displaystyle\|g_{T}*(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|

with respect to the state
\Phi_{\alpha}
. Estimates for the expression in Eq. (
4.7
), and other results concerning the issue of Bose-Einstein condensation and the closeness of
\Phi_{\alpha}
to a product state
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\otimes\Xi_{\varphi}
, see Eq. (
2.11
), are being verified in Section
9
and used in the subsequent Theorem
4
, which identifies the leading order behaviour of the expression in Eq. (
4.6
). For the convenience of the reader, we are going to state the main results of Section
9
, which have been verified in Lemma
9.2
, Lemma
9.3
and Lemma
9.4
respectively

(4.8)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},W^{-1}_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\mathcal{N}W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq C\alpha^{-\frac{2}{29}},

(4.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\!\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\Phi_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)\!\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\leq\alpha^{-\epsilon}T^{\frac{3}{2}},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \|Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha}\|\leq C\alpha^{-\epsilon},

where
Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}:=Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}
,
T>0
and
C,\epsilon>0
are suitable constants.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
H
be as in Eq. (
4.1
), and define on
L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
the linear operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(X):=X+2\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\langle\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},X\right\rangle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{j}.

Given a bounded function
f_{0}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
, let us furthermore choose
f:=g_{T}*f_{0}
in Eq. (
3.5
). Then there exist constants
\epsilon>0
and
C>0
such that for
T\geq 1
and
\Lambda\geq 1

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{-4}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\mathcal{L}\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f_{i}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right\|^{2}+C\left(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{\frac{3}{2}}\alpha^{-\epsilon}+\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\alpha^{-4}|p|^{2}.

Proof.

Let us define the auxiliary function
\zeta:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

(4.11)

\displaystyle\zeta(y):

\displaystyle=\left[p\!\cdot\!H(y)+p\!\cdot\!f(y)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int\partial_{y_{j}}(p\!\cdot\!f)\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{j}(y)\right]^{2},

\displaystyle\theta(Y_{n}):

\displaystyle=\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}-\alpha^{-4}\zeta(y_{n}),

which allow us to write

(4.12)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\zeta(y_{n})L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}+\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\theta(Y_{n})L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}.

Since the differences of the empirical measures satisfies
\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho_{Y_{n-1}}=\alpha^{-2}\delta_{y_{n}}
, we clearly have

\displaystyle\left[\int p\!\cdot\!(H+f)\mathrm{d}(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int\partial_{y_{j}}(p\!\cdot\!f)\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\!\!\int H_{j}\mathrm{d}(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}=\alpha^{-4}\zeta(y_{n}).

Furthermore,
\|\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\alpha^{-2}
. Consequently, we obtain by Lemma
4.1
, that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\theta(Y_{n})\right|\leq C\alpha^{-4}|p|^{2}\!\left(\|g\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\|g_{T}\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\alpha^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle\ \leq C\alpha^{-4}|p|^{2}\sqrt{\left(\|g\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n-1}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\|g_{T}\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n-1}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+2\alpha^{-2}\right)}

(4.13)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\sqrt{\left(\|g\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\|g_{T}\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+2\alpha^{-2}\right)},

for all
Y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3n}
satisfying that
(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}},\rho_{Y_{n-1}},\rho_{Y_{n}})
is an admissible triple in the sense of Lemma
5.2
. In the following we want to show that
(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}},\rho_{Y_{n-1}},\rho_{Y_{n}})
is an admissible triple for all
Y_{n}\in A_{n}
, where
A_{n}
is the set of all
Y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3n}
such that there exists a
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
with

(4.14)

\displaystyle(x,Y_{n-1}),(x,Y_{n})\in\mathrm{supp}(\Phi_{\alpha}),

with the concrete choices

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}:

\displaystyle=\underset{|y_{j}-x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})|\leq\ell}{\int}\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)=\underset{|y_{j}-x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})|\leq\ell}{\int}\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y),

\displaystyle\tilde{d}:

\displaystyle=\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}-\sigma-\kappa,

\displaystyle\tilde{D}:

\displaystyle=\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa,

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}:

\displaystyle=\delta_{*}+\sigma+\kappa,

\displaystyle\tilde{q}:

\displaystyle=q,\ \ \ \tilde{R}:=R_{*},\ \ \ \tilde{R}^{\prime}:=3,

for the constants in Lemma
5.2
, where
\kappa
,
R_{*}
,
\delta_{*}
,
\sigma
and
q
are chosen according to Subsection
2.4
. By our definition of
\tilde{\beta}
and the fact that
\sigma,\kappa>0
it is clear that
\nu:=\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}
satisfies

(4.15)

\displaystyle\tilde{d}\leq\int\mathrm{d}\nu\leq\tilde{D},\ \ \ \underset{|x-y|>\tilde{R}}{\int\int}\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\nu\leq\tilde{\delta},

and
\underset{|y_{j}-x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})|\leq\ell}{\int}\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)\geq\tilde{\beta}
. By the support properties of
\Phi_{\alpha}
we furthermore have
Y_{n}\in\Omega_{\sigma+\kappa}
and
Y_{n-1}\in\Omega_{\sigma+\kappa}
for
Y_{n}\in A_{n}
, and therefore Eq. (
4.15
) holds for
\nu\in\{\rho_{Y_{n}},\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\}
. We also note that
\tau_{\eta}(m_{\eta}(\nu))\neq 0
for
\nu\in\{\rho_{Y_{n}},\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\}
by the support properties of
\Phi_{\alpha}
, and hence
|m_{\eta}(\nu)|\leq\eta\leq 1
, see the definition of
\tau_{\eta}(\cdot)
below Eq. (
3.26
). Since
m_{q}\!\left(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)=0
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|m_{\eta}\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)-m_{\eta}\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right)\right|+\left|m_{\eta}\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right)-m_{\eta}\!\left(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\right|\leq 3=\tilde{R}^{\prime},

and conclude that
(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}},\rho_{Y_{n-1}},\rho_{Y_{n}})
is an admissible triple for
\alpha
large enough such that

\displaystyle\eta=\alpha^{-\beta}\leq\tilde{q}=q.

Especially, Eq. (
4.13
) holds for all
Y_{n}\in A_{n}
. Let us furthermore define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{\alpha}(Y_{n}):=\sqrt{\left(\|g\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\|g_{T}\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+2\alpha^{-2}\right)}\left|\Phi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})\right|.

By the support properties of
\Phi_{\alpha}
we have
\Phi_{\alpha}(x,Y_{n})=0
in case
n>(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa)\alpha^{2}:=D_{\alpha}
and hence we obtain together with the fact that Eq. (
4.13
) holds for all
Y_{n}\in A_{n}

(4.16)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\theta(Y_{n})L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq\!\!\!\sum_{n=1}^{D_{\alpha}}\!\!\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\alpha}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{A_{n}}\!\!\left|\Phi_{\alpha}(x,Y_{n})\right|\left|\Phi_{\alpha}(x,Y_{n-1})\right|\left|\theta(Y_{n})\right|\left|v^{\Lambda}_{x}(y_{n})\right|\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{-4}|p|^{2}\!\sum_{n=1}^{D_{\alpha}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\!\!\widetilde{\Phi}_{\alpha}(Y_{n})\widetilde{\Phi}_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})\!\left|v^{\Lambda}_{x}(y_{n})\right|\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\!\leq\!\sqrt{D+\kappa}\,C\alpha^{-4}|p|^{2}\|v^{\Lambda}\|\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{\alpha}\|^{2}\!\!.

Note that
\|v^{\Lambda}\|\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}
, see Lemma
2.1
, and by Eq. (
4.9
) there exists an
\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{\alpha}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left(\|g\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\|g_{T}\!*\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\!\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+2\alpha^{-2}\right)\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

(4.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{-\epsilon}+\alpha^{-\epsilon}T^{\frac{3}{2}}+\alpha^{-2}\lesssim\alpha^{-\epsilon}T^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Combining Eq. (
4.12
) with the estimates in Eq. (
4.16
) and Eq. (
4.17
) yields for a suitable
C>0

(4.18)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{-4}\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\zeta(y_{n})L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}+C\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{\frac{3}{2}}\alpha^{-(4+\epsilon)}|p|^{2}.

Recalling the definition of the operators
L
and
a^{*}
, we compute explicitly using the permutation symmetry of
\Phi_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\zeta(y_{n})L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\overline{\Phi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})}\Phi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})\zeta(y_{n})v^{\Lambda}_{x}(y_{n})\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\ =\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},L(\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}=\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},a^{*}(\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}

(4.19)

\displaystyle\ =\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}\right\rangle\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}+\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\Big(a^{*}(\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x})-\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}\right\rangle\Big)\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}.

Regarding the second term in Eq. (
4.19
), let us define
a^{*}_{j}:=a^{*}(u_{j})
for an orthonormal basis
\{u_{j}:j\in\mathbb{N}\}
of
L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
and estimate for
\lambda>0

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\Big(\!a^{*}(\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x})\!-\!\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}\right\rangle\!\Big)\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\!\!\!L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\!\!=\!\left\langle\!\Phi_{\alpha},\sum_{j}\braket{u_{j},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}}\left(a_{j}^{*}\!-\!\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},u_{j}\right\rangle\right)\!\Phi_{\alpha}\!\right\rangle_{\!\!\!\!L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}

(4.20)

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\lambda\!\left\langle\!\!\Phi_{\alpha},\!\sum_{j}\!|\!\braket{u_{j},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}}\!|^{2}\Phi_{\alpha}\!\!\right\rangle_{\!\!\!\!L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\!\!\!\!\!\!+\lambda^{-1}\!\!\left\langle\!\!\Phi_{\alpha},\!\sum_{j}\!\left(a_{j}^{*}\!-\!\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},u_{j}\right\rangle\right)\!\left(a_{j}\!-\!\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},u_{j}\right\rangle\right)\!\Phi_{\alpha}\!\!\right\rangle_{\!\!\!\!L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\!\!\!\!\!.

In order to analyse the term in Eq. (
4.20
), we use the Weyl operator
W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}
introduced in Eq. (
2.10
), in order to write

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\!\left(a_{j}^{*}\!-\!\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},u_{j}\right\rangle\right)\!\left(a_{j}\!-\!\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},u_{j}\right\rangle\right)=W^{-1}_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\mathcal{N}W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}},

and note that by Eq. (
4.8
) there exists a
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},W^{-1}_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\mathcal{N}W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq C\alpha^{-\frac{2}{29}}.

Since we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j}|\braket{u_{j},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}}|^{2}=\|\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}\|^{2}\lesssim|p|^{4}\|v^{\Lambda}_{x}\|^{2}=|p|^{4}\|v^{\Lambda}\|^{2}\lesssim\Lambda|p|^{4},

we obtain for the choice
\lambda:=\alpha^{-\frac{1}{29}}\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|p|^{-2}
and a suitable constant
C>0
the estimate

(4.21)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\Big(a^{*}(\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x})-\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}\right\rangle\Big)\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq C\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{29}}|p|^{2}.

In order to analyse the first term in Eq. (
4.19
), let us define the multiplication operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}:=\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}\right\rangle

acting on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and let us denote with
\mathcal{G}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}
the corresponding operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}
, which allows us to write

(4.22)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta v^{\Lambda}_{x}\right\rangle\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}=\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathcal{G}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle.

Recalling the definition of
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
,
P^{\mathrm{Pek}}
and
Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}
in Subsection
2.3
, let us define
P^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}:=P^{\mathrm{Pek}}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}
and
Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}:=Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}
, and compute

\displaystyle\ \ \ \left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathcal{G}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}=\left\langle\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\mathcal{G}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle-\|Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha}\|^{2}\left\langle\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\mathcal{G}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+2\mathfrak{Re}\left\langle P^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha},\mathcal{G}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}\,Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}+\left\langle Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha},\mathcal{G}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}\,Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}},

Together with the fact that the operator norm satisfies for a suitable
C>0

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\zeta\|_{\infty}\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|\,\|v^{\Lambda}\|\leq C|p|^{2}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}},

see Eq. (
2.18
), we obtain by Eq. (
4.10
) that there exists an
\epsilon>0
and a
D>0
such that

(4.23)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathcal{G}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}-\left\langle\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\mathcal{G}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle\right|\leq 4C|p|^{2}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\|Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha}\|\leq D\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{-\epsilon}|p|^{2}.

Combining the upper bound in Eq. (
4.18
) with the identities in Eq. (
4.19
) and Eq. (
4.22
), and the estimates in Eq. (
4.21
) and Eq. (
4.23
), yields for a suitable
C>0

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \leq\alpha^{-4}\left\langle\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\mathcal{G}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle+C\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{\frac{3}{2}}\alpha^{-(4+\epsilon)}|p|^{2}.

We use
\left|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}*v=\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
, see Lemma
2.1
, which tells us furthermore that

\displaystyle\left|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}*v^{\Lambda}=\chi(|\nabla|\leq\Lambda)\!\left(\left|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}*v\right)=\chi(|\nabla|\leq\Lambda)\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},

\displaystyle\ \ \ \left\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}-\left|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}*v^{\Lambda}\right\|=\|\chi(|\nabla|>\Lambda)\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|\leq\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}},

and therefore
\|\zeta\|_{\infty}\lesssim|p|^{2}
yields for a suitable
C>0

\displaystyle\left\langle\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\mathcal{G}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta\left|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}*v^{\Lambda}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle+C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|p|^{2}.

Finally, using
-\int\partial_{y_{j}}f\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}=-\int\partial_{y_{j}}f\!\left|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}\!\mathrm{d}x=2\int\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\mathrm{d}x
, we compute

\displaystyle\left\langle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\zeta\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\|p\!\cdot\!\left(H+f+2\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\mathrm{d}xH_{j}\right)\!\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\mathcal{L}\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f_{i}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right\|^{2}.

∎

Remark 4.3
.

In the following we want to choose the function
f=(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}):\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that it minimizes the following expression appearing in Theorem
4.2

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\mathcal{L}\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f_{i}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right\|^{2}.

Restricted to functions
\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f_{i}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, this minimization problem is clearly equivalent to the variational problem

(4.24)

\displaystyle\inf_{f:\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\mathcal{L}\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f_{i}\right)\!+\!\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right\|^{2}\!=\!\inf_{X\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\|\mathcal{L}\!\left(X\right)+Y\right\|^{2}\!=\!\|\pi Y\|^{2}\!,

with
Y:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}
and
\pi
being the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{L}\!\left(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)^{\perp}
. In order to identify
\mathcal{L}\!\left(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)
, note that
\mathcal{L}(X)=X
in case
X\perp\partial_{j}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
for all
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, hence

(4.25)

\displaystyle\left\{\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\partial_{y_{2}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\partial_{y_{3}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\}^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{L}\!\left(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right).

It turns out that the inclusion in Eq. (
4.25
) is even an identity. To verify this, recall that
\varphi\mapsto m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{\varphi})
is a boost in the sense of Eq. (
1.19
), which especially means that

\displaystyle m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{z}\right)=m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{z}}\right)=m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\right)+z=z,

where
\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{z}(x):=\left|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(x-z)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x
, and therefore as a consequence of Lemma
5.2

(4.26)

\displaystyle\delta_{i,j}=\partial_{z_{i}}z_{j}\Big|_{z=0}=\partial_{z_{i}}m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{z}\right)_{j}\Big|_{z=0}=-\int H_{j}\partial_{y_{i}}\left|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x=-2\braket{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{j},\partial_{y_{i}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}.

With this at hand we compute for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
and
X\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{y_{i}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\mathcal{L}(X)\right\rangle=\left\langle\partial_{y_{i}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},X\right\rangle+2\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\langle\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},X\right\rangle\left\langle\partial_{y_{i}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{j}\right\rangle=0.

Hence,
\mathcal{L}\!\left(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)=\left\{\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\partial_{y_{2}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\partial_{y_{3}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\}^{\perp}
, or equivalently
\pi
is the orthogonal projection onto
\mathrm{span}\!\left\{\partial_{y_{1}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\partial_{y_{2}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\partial_{y_{3}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\}
. Using again Eq. (
4.26
), we can therefore express the right hand side of Eq. (
4.24
) as

\displaystyle\|\pi Y\|^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{\left|\braket{\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},Y}\right|^{2}}{\|\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\braket{\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}}\right|^{2}}{\|\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{p_{j}^{2}}{4\|\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}}.

Due to the rotational symmetry of
\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
we have
\|\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{3}\|\nabla\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, and therefore we obtain by Eq. (
4.24
)

(4.27)

\displaystyle\inf_{f:\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\mathcal{L}\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f_{i}\right)\!+\!\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right\|^{2}=\frac{3|p|^{2}}{4\|\nabla\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}}=\frac{|p|^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{LP}}},

with the Landau-Pekar constant
m_{\mathrm{LP}}=\frac{2}{3}\|\nabla\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}
introduced in Eq. (
1.6
). Finally, we note that a (
p
-independent) optimizer of Eq. (
4.27
) is given by

\displaystyle f^{\diamond}_{i}:=\frac{(\pi-1)\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right)}{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}=-\frac{\partial_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}{m_{\mathrm{LP}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}-H_{i}.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Let us define the function
f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
in Eq. (
3.5
) as

\displaystyle f:=g_{T}*f^{\diamond}=-g_{T}*\!\left(\frac{\nabla\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}{m_{\mathrm{LP}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}+H\right),

and note that
f(-y)=-f(y)
, and therefore we have by Corollary
3.5

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha,p},\mathbb{H}_{p}\Psi_{\alpha,p}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\mu_{\alpha}^{2}\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\!\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\!L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle+|p|^{2}\mathcal{E}_{0}-2\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathfrak{Re}\!\left[\mathcal{E}_{j}\right],

where
\mu_{\alpha}\in(0,1]
is defined below Eq. (
3.26
). Since
f^{\diamond}\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
, see Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\leq\|f^{\diamond}\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla f^{\diamond}\|_{\infty}<C

for all
T>0
, and consequently we obtain using the estimates on
\mathcal{E}_{0},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{4}
from Lemma
3.4
, Lemma
8.2
, Lemma
8.3
and Lemma
8.4
that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}(p)\leq\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha,p},\mathbb{H}_{p}\Psi_{\alpha,p}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}\!+\!\mu_{\alpha}^{2}\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left[p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}L(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +C|p|^{2}\alpha^{-4}\left\{\alpha^{-1}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\alpha^{-2}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}|p|\right\}.

In combination with Theorem
4.2
and the fact that
0<\mu_{\alpha}\leq 1
we obtain for suitable constants
\epsilon^{\prime},\kappa,C>0
, and the concrete choice
\Lambda:=\alpha^{\kappa}
, for all
|p|\leq\alpha^{2-\epsilon^{\prime}}

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}(p)\leq E_{\alpha}+\alpha^{-4}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\mathcal{L}\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f_{i}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right\|^{2}+C|p|^{2}\alpha^{-(4+\epsilon^{\prime})}T^{\frac{3}{2}}.

In order to compare this with the right hand side of Eq. (
4.27
), we use that
f^{\diamond}
is an element of
C_{\mathrm{b}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R}^{3})
and therefore

\displaystyle\|f-f^{\diamond}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|y|T^{3}g(Ty)\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{C}{T},

for a suitable constant
C>0
. Since the operator
\mathcal{L}
is bounded and
\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
, we obtain for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\mathcal{L}\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f_{i}\right)\!+\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right\|^{2}\!\!\!\!\leq\!\!\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\mathcal{L}\!\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}f^{\diamond}_{i}\right)\!+\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{3}p_{i}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}H_{i}\right\|^{2}\!\!\!\!+\!\frac{C|p|^{2}}{T}\!=\!\frac{|p|^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{LP}}}\!+\!\frac{C|p|^{2}}{T}\!,

where we have used Eq. (
4.27
). Choosing
T:=\alpha^{\xi}
with
0<\frac{3}{2}\xi<\epsilon^{\prime}
and defining
\epsilon:=\min\left\{\xi,\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{3}{2}\xi\right\}
yields for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle E_{\alpha}(p)\leq E_{\alpha}+\frac{|p|^{2}}{2\alpha^{4}m_{\mathrm{LP}}}+C\alpha^{-(4+\epsilon)}|p|^{2}.

Recalling the definition of the effective mass
m_{\mathrm{eff}}(\alpha)
in Eq. (
1.6
), we therefore have

\displaystyle m_{\mathrm{eff}}(\alpha)=\lim_{p\rightarrow 0}\frac{|p|^{2}}{2\left(E_{\alpha}(p)-E_{\alpha}\right)}\geq\left(1+\frac{2C\alpha^{-\epsilon}}{m_{\mathrm{LP}}}\right)^{-1}\!\!\alpha^{4}m_{\mathrm{LP}}\geq\alpha^{4}m_{\mathrm{LP}}-2C\alpha^{4-\epsilon}.

∎

5.
Analysis of the generalized Median

It is the goal of this Section to analyse the regularized median, and especially to identify the leading order term of the increment

\displaystyle m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-m_{q}(\chi*\rho),

as
\rho^{\prime}\longrightarrow\rho
in a suitable topology, see Lemma
5.2
. Since the regularized median
m_{q}
involves the quantiles
x^{\lambda}
of the marginal measures
\rho_{j}
, we are first going to investigate in Lemma
5.1
the increments

\displaystyle x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}\right)-x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right).

In the following, it will be convenient to define for
t\in\mathbb{R}
and a permutation symmetric mollifier
\chi
, the function

(5.1)

\displaystyle f_{j,t}^{\chi}:\begin{cases}\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow[0,1],\\
y\mapsto(\chi*\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,t]}(\cdot_{j}))(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\chi(z_{j})\mathds{1}_{(-\infty,t]}(y_{j}-z_{j})\mathrm{d}z,\end{cases}

where we keep track of the function
\chi
used for the convolution in our notation. Clearly
\partial_{t}f_{j,t}^{\chi}=-\partial_{y_{j}}f_{j,t}^{\chi}\geq 0
and there exist constants
\ell,h>0
such that

(5.2)

\displaystyle-\partial_{y_{j}}f_{j,t}^{\chi}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})\geq h\mathds{1}_{(t-2\ell,t+2\ell)}(y_{j}).

Let us recall the definition of the quantile
x^{\lambda}(\nu)
of a measure
\nu
on
\mathbb{R}
in Eq. (
3.2
) as well as the definition of the marginal measure
\rho_{j}
in Eq. (
3.3
). Using the functions
f_{j,t}
we can express the
\lambda
-quantile of
(\chi*\rho)_{j}
as

\displaystyle x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right)=\sup\Big\{t:\int f_{j,t}^{\chi}\mathrm{d}\rho\leq\lambda\int\mathrm{d}\rho\Big\},

Since the marginals distributions of the convoluted measures
(\chi*\rho)_{j}
are atom-free, the supremum in the definition of
x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right)
is a maximum for all
0<\lambda<1
and we have

\displaystyle\int\Big(f_{j,x^{\lambda}\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right)}^{\chi}-\lambda\Big)\mathrm{d}\rho=\int_{-\infty}^{x^{\lambda}\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right)}\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho)_{j}-\lambda\int\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho)_{j}=0.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\chi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow[0,1]
be a
C^{\infty}
function with compact support and
\int\chi\,\mathrm{d}x=1
. Given
\beta>0
, there exist constants
\gamma,C>0
such that for all
0<\lambda<1
and measures
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
satisfying
\underset{|y_{j}-x^{\lambda}\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right)|\leq\ell}{\int}\mathrm{d}\rho(y)\geq\beta
and
\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\gamma

(5.3)

\displaystyle\left|x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}\right)-x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right)\right|\leq C\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.

Proof.

In order to keep the notation light let us write
x:=x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right)
and
x^{\prime}:=x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}\right)
. We first verify that there exists a constant
c>0
such that for
y\in(x-\ell,x+\ell)

(5.4)

\displaystyle c|y-x|\leq\left|\int(f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho\right|.

Making use of the assumption
\underset{|y_{1}-x^{\lambda}\left((\chi*\rho)_{j}\right)|\leq\ell}{\int}\mathrm{d}\rho\geq\beta
and Eq. (
5.2
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho\right|=\left|\int(f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho-\int(f_{j,x}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho\right|=|y\!-\!x|\int_{0}^{1}\int(-\partial_{y_{j}}f_{j,x+s(y-x)}^{\chi})\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \geq|y-x|h\int_{0}^{1}\underset{|y_{j}-x-s(y-x)|\leq 2\ell}{\int}\mathrm{d}\rho(y)\mathrm{d}s\geq|y-x|h\underset{|y_{j}-x|\leq\ell}{\int}\mathrm{d}\rho(y)\geq(h\beta)|y-x|,

which concludes the proof of Eq. (
5.4
). In order to prove Eq. (
5.3
), we are going to distinguish between the cases
x^{\prime}\geq x
and
x^{\prime}\leq x
. In the first case, we define
y:=\min\{x^{\prime},x+\ell\}\geq x
. Using
y\leq x^{\prime}
and Eq. (
5.4
), and the fact that
|f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda|\leq 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle 0\geq

\displaystyle\int(f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}\geq\int(f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho-\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\left|\int(f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho\right|-\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle\geq c|y-x|-\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=c(y-x)-\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}},

and consequently

(5.5)

\displaystyle\min\{|x^{\prime}-x|,\ell\}=y-x\leq\frac{1}{c}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.

Choosing
\gamma<c\ell
we have
\frac{1}{c}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}<\ell
, and therefore
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\frac{1}{c}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
by Eq. (
5.5
). Regarding the other case
x^{\prime}\leq x
we define
y:=\max\{x^{\prime},x-\ell\}
and proceed similarly

\displaystyle 0\leq\int(f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}\leq\int(f_{j,y}^{\chi}-\lambda)\mathrm{d}\rho+\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq c(y-x)+\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.

This again yields Eq. (
5.5
), which concludes the proof.
∎

In the following we want to quantify the increment
m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-m_{q}(\chi*\rho)
of the regularized median. For this purpose let us introduce the abbreviation

(5.6)

\displaystyle x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho):=x^{\frac{1}{2}\pm q}((\chi*\rho)_{j}),

and define for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
the functions
H_{j,q,\rho}^{\chi}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ H_{j,q,\rho}^{\chi}(y):=\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho}\Bigg(\left[\mathds{1}_{[x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho),x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)]}(y_{j})\big(y_{j}-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})\big)\right]*\chi

\displaystyle\!+\!\big(x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)\!-\!m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})\big)\!\left(\!\frac{1}{2}\!+\!q\!-\!f_{j,x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}(y)\!\!\right)\!+\!\big(m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})\!-\!x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)\big)\!\left(\frac{1}{2}\!-\!q\!-\!f_{j,x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}(y)\!\!\right)\!\!\!\!\Bigg),

where we keep track of
q,\rho
and
\chi
in our notation. Furthermore, we collect all the scalar valued functions
H_{j,q,\rho}^{\chi}
into a single vector valued function
H^{\chi}_{q,\rho}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
, i.e. we define

(5.7)

\displaystyle H_{q,\rho}^{\chi}(y):=(H_{1,q,\rho}^{\chi}(y),H_{2,q,\rho}^{\chi}(y),H_{3,q,\rho}^{\chi}(y)).

Following the convention introduced in Subsection
2.4
, let
\chi
be a convolution
\chi=g*g
, where
g
is a rotational symmetric mollifier. Then, Lemma
5.2
identifies the leading order of
m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})-m_{q}(\chi*\rho)
, up to an error depending on the
g
-mollified total variation.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\ell
be as in Eq. (
5.2
) for
f_{j,t}^{g}
. Given
\beta,q,d,D,\delta,R,R^{\prime}>0
, we call a triple
(\rho_{0},\rho,\rho^{\prime})
of (finite, Borel) measures admissible, in case there exists a
0<\tilde{q}\leq q
such that

(5.8)

\displaystyle d\leq\int\mathrm{d}\nu\leq D,\ \ \ \underset{|x-y|>R}{\int\int}\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\nu\leq\delta,\ \ \ |m_{\tilde{q}}(\rho^{\prime})-m_{\tilde{q}}(\rho)|+|m_{\tilde{q}}(\rho)-m_{\tilde{q}}(\rho_{0})|\leq R^{\prime},

for any
\nu\in\{\rho_{0},\rho,\rho^{\prime}\}
, and
\underset{|y_{j}-x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})|\leq\ell}{\int}\mathrm{d}\rho_{0}(y)\geq\beta
. Let
0<\delta<\frac{d^{2}}{2}
and
0<q\leq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta}{d^{2}}
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
, such that for all admissible triples

(5.9)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho)+\int H_{q,\rho_{0}}^{\chi}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)-m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|g\!*\!(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)\|_{TV}\!\left(\|g\!*\!(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)\|_{TV}+\|g\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho_{0})\|_{TV}\right).

Proof.

In a first step, we are going to verify for general convolution functions
\chi
satisfying Eq. (
5.2
) for some
h>0
, that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho)+\int H_{q,\rho_{0}}^{\chi}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)-m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})\right|\leq C\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}\!\left(\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}+\|\rho\!-\!\rho_{0}\|_{TV}\right).

Let us first identify
m_{q}((\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j})-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})
as

\displaystyle m_{q}((\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j})-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})=\frac{1}{\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}}\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}y\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y)-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\frac{1}{\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}}\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\Big[y-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})\Big]\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\frac{1}{\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}}\left(S_{1}+S_{2}-S_{3}\right),

where we define

\displaystyle S_{1}:

\displaystyle=\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}\Big[y-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})\Big]\mathrm{d}(\chi*(\rho^{\prime}-\rho))_{j}(y),

\displaystyle S_{2}:

\displaystyle=\int_{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\Big[y-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})\Big]\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y),

\displaystyle S_{2}:

\displaystyle=\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\Big[y-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})\Big]\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y).

Let us start with the observation that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\int_{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}=\int\left(\frac{1}{2}+q-f_{j,x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{\chi}\right)\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho),

(5.12)

\displaystyle\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}=\int\left(\frac{1}{2}-q-f_{j,x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{\chi}\right)\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho).

We can therefore write

\displaystyle m_{q}((\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j})-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})=\int H_{q,\rho}^{\chi}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)+\left(\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho}\right)\!\big(S_{1}+S_{2}-S_{3}\big)

(5.13)

\displaystyle\ \ +\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho}\left(\int_{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\Big[y-x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)\Big]\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y)-\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\Big[y-x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)\Big]\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y)\right).

In the following let
r>0
be large enough such that
\mathrm{supp}(\chi)\subseteq B_{r}(0)
, and note that we have for measures
\nu\neq 0

\displaystyle\left|x^{\lambda}\!\left((\chi*\nu)_{j}\right)-x^{\lambda}\!\left(\nu\right)\right|\leq r.

Furthermore, using
[
4
, Lemma 3.9]
together with our assumptions on
\nu\in\{\rho_{0},\rho,\rho^{\prime}\}
yields

\displaystyle\left|x^{\frac{1}{2}+q}\!\left(\nu\right)-x^{\frac{1}{2}-q}\!\left(\nu\right)\right|\leq 2R.

In combination with the assumption
|m_{\tilde{q}}(\rho^{\prime})-m_{\tilde{q}}(\rho)|+|m_{\tilde{q}}(\rho)-m_{\tilde{q}}(\rho_{0})|\leq R^{\prime}
, and the fact that
x^{\frac{1}{2}-q}\!\left(\nu\right)\leq x^{\frac{1}{2}-\tilde{q}}\!\left(\nu\right)\leq m_{\tilde{q}}(\nu)\leq x^{\frac{1}{2}+\tilde{q}}\!\left(\nu\right)\leq x^{\frac{1}{2}+q}\!\left(\nu\right)
, we obtain

(5.14)

\displaystyle|x-y|\leq\widehat{R}:=R^{\prime}+2r+4R,\ \mathrm{for}\ x,y\in\bigcup_{\nu\in\{\rho_{0},\rho,\rho^{\prime}\}}\{x^{-}_{j,q}(\nu),m_{q}((\chi*\nu)_{j}),x^{+}_{j,q}(\nu)\}.

As a consequence we can estimate
\left|S_{1}\right|\leq\widehat{R}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
and for
q\in\{2,3\}

\displaystyle\left|S_{q}\right|\leq\widehat{R}\int_{x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}\leq\widehat{R}\left\|\frac{1}{2}\pm q-f_{j,x^{+}_{j,q}}^{\chi}\right\|_{\infty}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\widehat{R}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}},

where we have used Eq. (
5.11
) and Eq. (
5.12
). Consequently

(5.15)

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho}\right)\!\big(S_{1}+S_{2}-S_{3}\big)\right|\leq\frac{3\widehat{R}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}}{4q^{2}}\frac{\left|\int\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)\right|}{\int\mathrm{d}\rho\int\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}}\leq\frac{3\widehat{R}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|^{2}_{\mathrm{TV}}}{4q^{2}d^{2}}.

Similarly, we can use Eq. (
5.11
) and Eq. (
5.11
) in order to estimate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho}\left(\int_{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\Big[y-x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)\Big]\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y)-\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\Big[y-x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)\Big]\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y)\right)

(5.16)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \leq\frac{\left|x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})-x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)\right|+\left|x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})-x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)\right|}{2qd}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\frac{\widehat{R}}{qd}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.

By Eq. (
5.14
) we have
\|H_{q,\rho}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{3\widehat{R}}{2qd}
. Combining Eq. (
5.13
), Eq. (
5.15
) and Eq. (
5.16
) yields

\displaystyle\left|m_{q}((\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j})-m_{q}((\chi*\rho)_{j})\right|\leq\left(\|H_{q,\rho}\|_{\infty}+\frac{3\widehat{R}\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}}{4q^{2}d^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{R}}{qd}\right)\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq\left(\frac{3\widehat{R}}{2qd}+\frac{3\widehat{R}D}{4q^{2}d^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{R}}{qd}\right)\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.

Consequently, it is clearly enough to verify Eq. (
5.10
) in the case that
\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}\leq\gamma
and
\|\rho\!-\!\rho_{0}\|_{TV}\leq\gamma
, where we choose
\gamma>0
as in Lemma
5.1
. Assuming

\displaystyle\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{TV}\leq\gamma,

\displaystyle\|\rho\!-\!\rho_{0}\|_{TV}\leq\gamma,

we have by Lemma
5.1
that
\left|x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})-x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho)\right|\leq C\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
, which yields in combination with Eq. (
5.16
)

(5.18)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q\!\int\mathrm{d}\rho}\!\left(\!\int_{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\!\!\Big[y\!-\!x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)\!\Big]\!\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y)\!-\!\int_{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho^{\prime})}\!\!\Big[y\!-\!x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)\!\Big]\!\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})_{j}(y)\right)\!\leq\!\frac{C}{qd}\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|^{2}_{\mathrm{TV}}.

Finally, we need to analyse the term

(5.19)

\displaystyle\left|\int H_{q,\rho}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)-\int H_{q,\rho_{0}}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}-\rho)\right|\leq\|H_{q,\rho}-H_{q,\rho_{0}}\|_{\infty}\|\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.

By our assumption
\|\rho\!-\!\rho_{0}\|_{TV}\leq\gamma
, we can apply Lemma
5.1
, which yields

(5.20)

\displaystyle\left|x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho)-x^{\pm}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})\right|\leq C\|\rho\!-\!\rho_{0}\|_{TV}.

As an immediate consequence of Eq. (
5.17
) and Eq. (
5.20
), we obtain for a suitable
C>0

\displaystyle\left|H^{(j)}_{q,\rho}(y)-H^{(j)}_{q,\rho_{0}}(y)\right|\leq C\|\rho-\rho_{0}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\frac{1}{2q\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{0}}\big(|B_{1}|+|B_{2}|+|B_{3}|+|B_{4}|\big),

with

\displaystyle B_{1}:

\displaystyle=\left[\mathds{1}_{[x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho_{0}),x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)]}(y_{j})\big(y_{j}-m_{q}((\chi*\rho_{0})_{j})\big)\right]*\chi,

\displaystyle B_{2}:

\displaystyle=\left[\mathds{1}_{[x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho_{0}),x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)]}(y_{j})\big(y_{j}-m_{q}((\chi*\rho_{0})_{j})\big)\right]*\chi,

\displaystyle B_{3}:

\displaystyle=\big(x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})-m_{q}((\chi*\rho_{0})_{j})\big)\!\left(f_{j,x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})}^{\chi}(y)-f_{j,x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{\chi}(y)\right),

\displaystyle B_{4}:

\displaystyle=\big(x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})-m_{q}((\chi*\rho_{0})_{j})\big)\!\left(f_{j,x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})}^{\chi}(y)-f_{j,x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho)}^{\chi}(y)\right).

Let us analyse the term
B_{1}
in detail, the other terms can be controlled similarly. By Eq. (
5.14
), we have for all
y
such that
x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})\leq y_{j}\leq x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)

\displaystyle|y_{j}-m_{q}((\chi*\rho_{0})_{j})|\leq x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)-x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})\leq\widehat{R},

and therefore we obtain with the aid of Eq. (
5.20
) for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\ \ |B_{1}|\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\chi(y-z)\mathds{1}_{[x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho_{0}),x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)]}(z_{j})\left|z_{j}-m_{q}((\chi*\rho_{0})_{j})\right|\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{R}\left|x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho)-x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho_{0})\right|\sup_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\chi(t,u,v)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\leq C\|\rho\!-\!\rho_{0}\|_{TV}.

Similarly we obtain
|B_{j}|\leq C\|\rho-\rho_{0}\|_{TV}
for
j\in\{2,3,4\}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\|H_{q,\rho}-H_{q,\rho_{0}}\|_{\infty}\leq 4C\|\rho-\rho_{0}\|_{TV},

which concludes the proof of Eq. (
5.10
) by Eq. (
5.19
).

In order to verify Eq. (
5.9
) for the specific convolution function
\chi=g*g
introduced in Subsection
2.4
, note that the measures
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}:=g*\rho_{0}
,
\widetilde{\rho}:=g*\rho
and
\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}:=g*\rho
are an admissible triple with
\widetilde{R}:=R+2\widetilde{r}
and
\widetilde{R}^{\prime}:=R^{\prime}+2\widetilde{r}
, where
g(x)=0
for
|x|\geq\widetilde{r}
, and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\beta}:=\beta\inf_{x:|x_{j}|\leq\ell}\int_{|y_{j}|\leq\ell}g(y-x)\mathrm{d}y>0.

Consequently, we obtain by Eq. (
5.10
), used for the convolution function
\widetilde{\chi}:=g
,

\displaystyle\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho)+\int H_{q,\rho_{0}}^{\chi}\mathrm{d}(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)-m_{q}(\chi*\rho^{\prime})\right|=\left|m_{q}(g*\widetilde{\rho})+\int H_{q,\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}^{g}\mathrm{d}(\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho})-m_{q}(g*\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq C\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho}\|_{TV}\!\left(\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho}\|_{TV}+\|\widetilde{\rho}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{TV}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =C\|g\!*\!(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)\|_{TV}\!\left(\|g\!*\!(\rho^{\prime}\!-\!\rho)\|_{TV}+\|g\!*\!(\rho\!-\!\rho_{0})\|_{TV}\right).

∎

6.
Spatial concentration of Probability

It is the objective of this Section to derive decay properties for the probability measure
|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}
. To be more precise, we show in Theorem
6.1
that moments of the regularized median
m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})
are bounded (uniformly in
\alpha
) on the set of non-degenerate configurations
Y_{n}
satisfying
\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d
. The fact that the statistical quantity
m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})
has bounded moments on
\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d
will be a central tool in Section
8
, where we analyse the various residual terms
\mathcal{E}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{4}
that have been separated from the energy
\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha,p},\mathbb{H}_{p}\Psi_{\alpha,p}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}
in Subsection
3.2
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
0<q<\frac{1}{2}
,
d>0
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists a
C<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|^{m}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq C.

Before we come to the proof of Theorem
6.1
, we need the auxiliary Lemma
6.2
, Lemma
6.3
and Lemma
6.4
, which will provide us with strong bounds on the probabilities

(6.1)

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(s,\lambda):=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\underset{\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{s,\lambda}^{(n)}}{\int}|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}Y_{n},

where
\Omega_{s,\lambda}^{(n)}
is the set of all
Y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3n}
such that

\displaystyle\int\chi(|y|>s)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}<\lambda.

Note that
\Omega_{s,\lambda}^{(n)}
depends on
\alpha
as well via the empirical measure
\rho_{Y_{n}}
defined in Eq. (
2.1
). Let us furthermore introduce
\Omega_{s,\lambda}:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\Omega_{s,\lambda}^{(n)}
and the shifted version
\Omega^{(n)}_{s,\lambda}(x)
as the set of all
Y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3n}
satisfying

\displaystyle\int\chi(|y-x|>s)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}<\lambda.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
\lambda>0
, there exists a constant
s_{0}
such that for all
s\geq s_{0}
and all states
\Phi\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}
with
\mathrm{supp}(\Phi)\subseteq\underset{n\in\mathbb{N},x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}{\bigcup}\{x\}\times\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{s,\lambda}^{(n)}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi,\mathbb{H}\Phi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq E_{\alpha}+\frac{\lambda}{4}.

Proof.

Following the approach in
[
18
]
, respectively
[
4
, Theorem 2.5]
, let us localize the electron coordinate
x
by introducing the states

\displaystyle\Phi_{x^{\prime}}(x;Y_{n}):=\tau_{s}(x-x^{\prime})\Phi(x;Y_{n}),

where
\tau_{s}(y):=s^{-\frac{3}{2}}\tau(s^{-1}y)
and
\tau
is a smooth
[0,1]
-valued function with
\tau(y)=0
in case
|y|>\frac{1}{3}
and
\int\tau(y)^{2}\mathrm{d}y=1
, see Subsection
2.4
. It is elementary to check that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi,\mathbb{H}\Phi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\langle\Phi_{x^{\prime}},\mathbb{H}\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-s^{-2}\left\|\nabla\tau\right\|^{2},

see for example
[
4
, Theorem 2.5]
. In order to analyse
\left\langle\Phi_{x^{\prime}},\mathbb{H}\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}
, let us introduce

\displaystyle v^{\leq}_{x,x^{\prime},s}(y):

\displaystyle=\chi\!\left(|y-x^{\prime}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\right)v_{x}(y),

\displaystyle v^{>}_{x,x^{\prime},s}(y):

\displaystyle=v_{x}(y)-v^{\leq}_{x,x^{\prime},s}(y)=\chi\!\left(|y-x^{\prime}|>\frac{s}{2}\right)v_{x}(y),

as well as the multiplication operators
\mathcal{N}^{\leq}_{x^{\prime},s}=\mathcal{N}^{\leq}_{x^{\prime},s}(\rho_{Y_{n}})
and
\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}=\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}(\rho_{Y_{n}})
, and the operator
\mathbb{H}_{x^{\prime},s}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\leq}_{x^{\prime},s}(\rho_{Y_{n}}):

\displaystyle=\int\chi\!\left(|y-x^{\prime}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}(\rho_{Y_{n}}):

\displaystyle=\int\chi\!\left(|y-x^{\prime}|>\frac{s}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{x^{\prime},s}:=-\Delta_{x}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{N}^{\leq}_{x^{\prime},s}-a\!\left(v^{\leq}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\right)^{*}-a\!\left(v^{\leq}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\right).

Using the set
A_{x^{\prime}}:=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\frac{s}{2}\right\}
we can split the space
L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}
into the tensor factors

(6.3)

\displaystyle L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}\cong L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}\!\left(A_{x^{\prime}}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus A_{x^{\prime}}\right),

and we observe that the operator
\mathbb{H}_{x^{\prime},s}
acts as the identity on the last factor in the decomposition in Eq. (
6.3
). Denoting with
\Omega_{x^{\prime}}
the vacuum in
\mathcal{F}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus A_{x^{\prime}}\right)
, we furthermore have for any state
\Phi^{\prime}\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}\!\left(A_{x^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{x^{\prime}},\mathbb{H}_{x^{\prime},s}\Phi^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}=\left\langle\Phi^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{x^{\prime}},\mathbb{H}\Phi^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq E_{\alpha},

and therefore we have as an operator inequality

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{x^{\prime},s}\geq E_{\alpha}.

Making use of the decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}=\mathbb{H}_{x^{\prime},s}+\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}-a\!\left(v^{>}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\right)^{*}-a\!\left(v^{>}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\right)

we obtain by Eq. (
6.4
)

(6.5)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{x^{\prime}},\mathbb{H}\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq E_{\alpha}\|\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\|^{2}+\left\langle\Phi_{x^{\prime}},\left(\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}-a\!\left(v^{>}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\right)^{*}-a\!\left(v^{>}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\right)\right)\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}.

Note that
\Phi_{x^{\prime}}(x;Y_{n})=0
for
|x-x^{\prime}|>\frac{s}{3}
and that for
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\frac{s}{3}

\displaystyle\|v^{>}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\|^{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\chi\!\left(|y-x^{\prime}|>\frac{s}{2}\right)}{|y-x|^{4}}\mathrm{d}y\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\chi\!\left(|y-x|>\frac{s}{6}\right)}{|y-x|^{4}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim s^{-1},

and therefore there exists a constant
C>0
such that we have for all
(x;Y_{n})\in\mathrm{supp}(\Phi_{x^{\prime}})
the operator inequality on
\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}-a\!\left(v^{>}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\right)^{*}-a\!\left(v^{>}_{x,x^{\prime},s}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}-Cs^{-1}.

Together with Eq. (
6.5
) we therefore obtain

(6.6)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{x^{\prime}},\mathbb{H}\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq\left(E_{\alpha}-Cs^{-1}\right)\|\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\Phi_{x^{\prime}},\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}.

We observe that
\Phi_{x^{\prime}}(x;Y_{n})\neq 0
implies
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\frac{s}{3}
as well as
Y_{n}\notin\Omega^{(n)}_{s,\lambda}(x)
and hence

\displaystyle\int\chi\!\left(|y-x^{\prime}|>\frac{s}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq\int\chi\!\left(|y-x|>s\right)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq\lambda.

Consequently,
\left\langle\Phi_{x^{\prime}},\mathcal{N}^{>}_{x^{\prime},s}\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq\lambda\|\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\|^{2}
, which yields together with Eq. (
6.2
) and Eq. (
6.6
) for a suitable constant
C>0
and
s
large enough

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi,\mathbb{H}\Phi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(E_{\alpha}+\frac{\lambda}{2}-Cs^{-1}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\Phi_{x^{\prime}}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-s^{-2}\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}

\displaystyle=E_{\alpha}+\frac{\lambda}{2}-Cs^{-1}-s^{-2}\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}\geq E_{\alpha}+\frac{\lambda}{4}.

∎

Let us define
g(y):=\chi_{1}(|y|^{2}>2)
. In the following Lemma
6.3
, we are going to quantify the localization error with respect to the localization functions

\displaystyle K_{1,s,\lambda,u}(\rho):

\displaystyle=\chi_{u}\!\left(\int\!g\!\left(s^{-1}y\right)\mathrm{d}\rho(y)\leq\lambda\right),

\displaystyle K_{2,s,\lambda,u}(\rho):

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-K_{1,s,\lambda,u}(\rho)^{2}}.

Lemma 6.3
.

For any
\lambda,u,z>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
s\geq 1

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}+CP_{\alpha}(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z}s^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We start with the observation that
[K_{j,s,\lambda,u},\mathcal{N}]=0
and therefore

(6.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}=\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}.

Furthermore, we can write

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})a(v)^{*}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},a(v)^{*}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ =\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(\sum_{j=1}^{2}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-1\right)L(v)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\ \ \ =\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},K_{*}(Y_{n})L(v)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)},

with
K_{*}:\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined as

\displaystyle K_{*}(Y_{n}):=\sum_{j=1}^{2}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-1=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})-K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}.

Trivially we have the inequality
|K_{*}|\leq 1
. We note that
K_{*}(Y_{n})\neq 0
implies
g\!\left(s^{-1}y_{n}\right)\neq 0
and therefore
|y_{n}|\geq s
. Furthermore,
K_{*}(Y_{n})\neq 0
implies either
\int\!g\!\left(s^{-1}y\right)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}(y)>\lambda-u
or
\int\!g\!\left(s^{-1}y\right)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}(y)>\lambda-u
, hence

\displaystyle Y_{n-1},Y_{n}\in A:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{s,\lambda-2u}

for
\alpha
large enough such that
\alpha^{-2}\leq u
. Consequently, we have the estimate

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},K_{*}(Y_{n})L(v)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}|\mathds{1}_{A}(Y_{n-1})\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})|\,|\mathds{1}_{A}(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|\,\chi(|y_{n}|>s)v(y_{n})\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

(6.9)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\,\left\|\chi(|\cdot|>s)v\right\|\,\left\|\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|.

Let us compute first

(6.10)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=P(s,\lambda-2u)\leq P(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z},

(6.11)

\displaystyle\left\|\chi(|\cdot|>s)v\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\chi(|y|>s)v(y)^{2}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\int\frac{\chi(|y|>s)}{|y|^{4}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim s^{-1}.

Furthermore, we obtain by Hölder’s inequality for the measure
|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}
and the random variables
Y_{n}\mapsto\frac{n}{\alpha^{2}}
and
Y_{n}\mapsto\mathds{1}_{A}(Y_{n})
, defined on the space
\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}
, for all
z>0

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|^{2}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\frac{n}{\alpha^{2}}\mathds{1}_{A}(Y_{n})\,|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\mathds{1}_{A}(Y_{n})^{\frac{1}{1-z}}\,|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\right)^{1-z}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\left(\frac{n}{\alpha^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{z}}\,|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\right)^{z}

(6.12)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ =P(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z}\braket{\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\frac{1}{z}}\Psi_{\alpha}}_{\mathcal{F}}^{z}\lesssim P(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z},

where we have used
\braket{\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\frac{1}{z}}\Psi_{\alpha}}_{\mathcal{F}}\lesssim 1
in the final estimate, see Lemma
7.2
. Combining Eq. (
6.9
), Eq. (
6.10
), Eq. (
6.11
) and Eq. (
6.12
) yields for a suitable constant
C>0

(6.13)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},K_{*}(Y_{n})L(v)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|\leq CP(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z}s^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Finally, we have to understand the localization error of the
\mathcal{P}^{2}
term in
\mathbb{H}
. For this purpose we apply the IMS formula again, see
[
16
, Theorem A.1]
, respectively
[
14
, Proposition 6.1]
and
[
4
, Lemma 3.3]
, in order to compute

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left[\left[K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}}),\mathcal{P}^{2}\right],K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right]\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

(6.14)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left[\mathcal{P},K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right]^{2}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}},

where we have used that
K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})
commutes with the multiplication operator

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{P},\!K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right]\!=\!\frac{1}{i}\!\sum_{k=1}^{n}\partial_{y_{k}}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!=\!\frac{1}{is}\Theta_{j}\!\left(\int\!g\!\left(s^{-1}y\right)\!\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}(y)\right)\!\!\int\!\nabla g\!\left(s^{-1}y\right)\!\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}(y),

and
\Theta_{j}
is defined as

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}(z):

\displaystyle=\nabla_{z}\chi_{u}(z\leq\lambda),

\displaystyle\Theta_{2}(z):

\displaystyle=\nabla_{z}\sqrt{1-\chi_{u}(z\leq\lambda)^{2}}.

We observe that
\Theta_{j}\!\left(\int\!g\!\left(s^{-1}y\right)\mathrm{d}\rho(y)\right)\neq 0
implies
\int\!g\!\left(s^{-1}y\right)\mathrm{d}\rho(y)\geq\lambda-u>\lambda-2u
, and therefore
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{s,\lambda-2u}
or equivalently
Y_{n}\in A=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{s,\lambda-2u}
. Applying again Hölder’s inequality yields for
z>0

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left[\mathcal{P},K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right]^{2}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\right|=\left|\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathds{1}_{A}\left[\mathcal{P},K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right]^{2}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathds{1}_{A}^{\frac{1}{1-z}}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}^{1-z}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left[\mathcal{P},K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right]^{\frac{2}{z}}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}^{z}

\displaystyle\leq s^{-2}P(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|\nabla\Theta_{j}\|^{2}_{\infty}\right)\|\nabla g\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)^{\frac{2}{z}}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}^{z}

(6.15)

\displaystyle=s^{-2}P(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|\nabla\Theta_{j}\|^{2}_{\infty}\right)\|\nabla g\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\frac{2}{z}}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}^{z}\lesssim s^{-2}P(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z},

where we have used
\braket{\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\frac{2}{z}}\Psi_{\alpha}}_{\mathcal{F}}\lesssim 1
, see Lemma
7.2
, which we are going to prove in the subsequent Section
7
. Combining Eq. (
6.7
), Eq. (
6.8
), Eq. (
6.13
), Eq. (
6.14
) and Eq. (
6.15
) yields for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\!+\!\sum_{j=1}^{2}\!\!\left\langle K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\!-\!2\sum_{j=1}^{2}\!\mathfrak{Re}\!\left\langle K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},a(v)^{*}K_{j,s,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\ \ \leq\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-2\mathfrak{Re}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},a(v)^{*}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+CP(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z}\!\left(s^{-\frac{1}{2}}+s^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ =E_{\alpha}+CP(s,\lambda-2u)^{1-z}\!\left(s^{-\frac{1}{2}}+s^{-2}\right).

∎

Combining Lemma
6.2
and Lemma
6.3
, we can provide strong bounds on the probability
P_{\alpha}(s,\lambda)
in the following Lemma
6.4
, which we will use together with a layer-cake representation to verify Theorem
6.1
.

Lemma 6.4
.

For any
\lambda,\gamma>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
s>0

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(s,\lambda)\leq Cs^{-\gamma}.

Proof.

Note that
P_{\alpha}(s,\lambda)
is a probability, i.e.
P_{\alpha}(s,\lambda)\leq 1
. By induction, it is therefore enough to verify that for any
\lambda,u,z>0
there exist constants
C_{,}s_{0}
such that for all
s\geq s_{0}

(6.16)

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(\sqrt{3}s,\lambda+3u)\leq CP_{\alpha}(s,\lambda)^{1-z}s^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Let us define the functions
K_{j}:=K_{j,s,\lambda+2u,u}
, introduced above Lemma
6.3
, and note that

(6.17)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle K_{j}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}K_{j}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}+CP_{\alpha}(s,\lambda)^{1-z}s^{-\frac{1}{2}},

by Lemma
6.3
for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
. Furthermore, we introduce the state

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}:=\frac{1}{\|K_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|}K_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}.

We observe that
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{\sqrt{3}s,\lambda+3u}
implies that

\displaystyle\int g(s^{-1}y)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}(y)\geq\int\chi(|y|>\sqrt{3}s)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}(y)\geq\lambda+3u,

and hence
K_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})=\sqrt{1-K_{1,s,\lambda+2u,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})^{2}}=0
for
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{\sqrt{3}s,\lambda+3u}
. We deduce that

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(\sqrt{3}s,\lambda+3u)\leq\|K_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}=1-\|K_{1}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}.

Consequently, we obtain together with Eq. (
6.17
) and the operator inequality
\mathbb{H}\geq E_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ P_{\alpha}(\sqrt{3}s,\lambda+3u)\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\left(\mathbb{H}_{0}-E_{\alpha}\right)\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|K_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\left(\mathbb{H}_{0}-E_{\alpha}\right)\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq CP_{\alpha}(s,\lambda)^{1-z}s^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

In order to conclude the proof of Eq. (
6.16
), we are going to verify that

(6.18)

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\left(\mathbb{H}_{0}-E_{\alpha}\right)\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\geq\frac{\lambda}{8},

for
\alpha
large enough by contradiction. In the following we will assume that Eq. (
6.18
) is violated, i.e. we assume

(6.19)

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}<E_{\alpha}+\frac{\lambda}{8}.

We further introduce the auxiliary states
\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}\subseteq L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)
as

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n}):=\tau_{\epsilon}(x)\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}(y_{1}-x,\dots,y_{n}-x),

where
\tau_{\epsilon}(x)=\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\tau\!\left(\epsilon x\right)
and
\tau
is a
[0,1]
-valued smooth function with compact support and
\int\tau^{2}\mathrm{d}x=1
, see Subsection
2.4
. Observe that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left\langle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha},(-\Delta_{x})\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}=\left\langle\tau_{\epsilon}\otimes\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\left(\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}-\mathcal{P}\right)^{2}\tau_{\epsilon}\otimes\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\left(1+\epsilon^{-1}\right)\!\left\langle\tau_{\epsilon},(-\Delta_{x})\tau_{\epsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)}\leq\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+C^{\prime}\!\left(\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right),

where we have used the assumption in Eq. (
6.19
) and the fact that
\mathcal{P}^{2}\leq 2\mathbb{H}_{0}+C
for a suitable constant
C>0
, see Eq. (
7.1
). Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left\langle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha},\Big\{\mathcal{N}-a^{*}(v_{x})-a(v_{x})\Big\}\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}=\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\Big\{\mathcal{N}-a^{*}(v)-a(v)\Big\}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}.

Therefore we obtain for
\epsilon
small enough such that
C^{\prime}\!\left(\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right)\leq\frac{\lambda}{8}

(6.20)

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\frac{\lambda}{8}<E_{\alpha}+\frac{\lambda}{4}.

Note that
\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n})\neq 0
implies that
\widetilde{Y}_{n}:=(y_{1}-x,\dots,y_{n}-x)
satisfies
K_{2}(\widetilde{Y}_{n})\neq 0
, and therefore
\widetilde{Y}_{n}\notin\Omega^{(n)}_{s,\lambda}
, or equivalently
Y_{n}\notin\Omega^{(n)}_{s,\lambda}(x)
. Consequently,
\widehat{\Phi}
satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
6.2
, which yields the desired contradiction to Eq. (
7.23
)

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq E_{\alpha}+\frac{\lambda}{4},

and therefore conclude the proof of Eq. (
6.18
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Since the case
m=0
is trivial, we are going to assume in the following that
m\geq 1
. Defining
A^{n}
as the set of all
Y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3n}
such that
\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d
, we obtain

(6.21)

\displaystyle\ \ \left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|^{m}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\geq d\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{A^{n}}\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|^{m}|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{A^{n}}\int_{0}^{\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|^{m}}\!\!|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\int_{0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{A^{n}}\chi\!\left(t^{\frac{1}{m}}\leq\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|\right)|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\mathrm{d}t

using a layer-cake representation. In the following let
R
be large enough such that
\chi(y)=0
in case
|x|>R
and let us show that for any
Y_{n}\in A^{n}
,
|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\geq s
implies

(6.22)

\displaystyle\int\chi\!\left(|y|\geq\frac{s}{3}-R\right)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}-q\right)d.

In order to prove Eq. (
6.22
), observe that in the case
|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\geq s
, at least one component
