\displaystyle m_{q}(\rho_{Y_{n}})=\left(m_{q}\left((\rho_{Y_{n}})_{1}\right),m_{q}\left((\rho_{Y_{n}})_{2}\right),m_{q}\left((\rho_{Y_{n}})_{3}\right)\right)

satisfies
|m_{q}\left((\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})_{j}\right)|\geq\frac{s}{3}
. W.l.o.g. assume
m_{q}\left((\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})_{j}\right)\geq\frac{s}{3}
. Therefore the quantile satisfies
x^{\frac{1}{2}+q}\left((\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})_{j}\right)\geq\frac{s}{3}
, which implies

\displaystyle\int\chi\!\left(|y|\geq\frac{s}{3}-R\right)\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq\int\chi\!\left(|y|\geq\frac{s}{3}\right)\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})\geq\int\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})-\int\chi\!\left(y<\frac{s}{3}\right)\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \geq\int\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})-\left(\frac{1}{2}+q\right)\int\mathrm{d}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})=\left(\frac{1}{2}-q\right)\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}-q\right)d.

Having Eq. (
6.22
) at hand for any
Y_{n}\in A^{n}
with
|m_{q}(\rho_{Y_{n}})|\geq s
, yields together with the layer-cake representation Eq. (
6.21
), the definition of the probability
P_{\alpha}(s,\lambda)
in Eq. (
6.1
) and
\lambda_{*}:=\left(\frac{1}{2}-q\right)d>0
the upper bound

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|^{m}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\geq d\right)\!\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\!P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{1}{3}t^{\frac{1}{m}}-R,\lambda_{*}\right)\mathrm{d}t.

Choosing
\gamma:=2m
in Lemma
6.4
gives us the estimate

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{1}{3}t^{\frac{1}{m}}-R,\lambda_{*}\right)\leq Ct^{-2}

for
t\geq t_{0}
and suitable constants
C,t_{0}>0
. Together with the observation that
P_{\alpha}\!\left(s,\lambda_{*}\right)\leq 1
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
we conclude

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\!P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{1}{3}t^{\frac{1}{m}}-R,\lambda_{*}\right)\mathrm{d}t\leq t_{0}+C\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}t^{-2}\mathrm{d}t=t_{0}+\frac{C}{t_{0}}.

∎

7.
Asymptotic concentration of Probability

In the following we want to control moments of
\mathcal{N}
in the ground state
\Psi_{\alpha}
, see Lemma
7.2
, and demonstrate that the probability measure
|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}
is mostly supported on sets of the form
\Omega_{\lambda}
defined in Eq. (
2.22
), for
\lambda>0
, as
\alpha\rightarrow\infty
goes to infinity, see Lemma
7.5
. The asymptotic concentration of
|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}
, as stated in Lemma
7.5
, is an important input for Lemma
3.4
, where we show essential bounds for the residual term
\mathcal{E}_{0}
, and Lemma
8.4
, where we show corresponding bounds for the residual term
\mathcal{E}_{4}
. In preparation for Lemma
7.2
, we first establish an upper bound on the particle number
\mathcal{N}
in terms of the fiber Hamilton operator
\mathbb{H}_{0}
in the subsequent Lemma
7.1
. The proof is based on the strategy developed in
[
18
]
, see also
[
4
]
.

Lemma 7.1
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for
K>0

(7.1)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \mathcal{P}^{2}+\mathcal{N}\leq 2\mathbb{H}_{0}+C,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\ \ -\Delta_{x}+\mathcal{N}\leq 2\mathbb{H}^{K}+C,

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{0}^{K}\leq\left(1+CK^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\mathbb{H}_{0}+K^{-\frac{1}{2}}C.

Furthermore, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C
such that

(7.4)

\displaystyle\pm\left(\mathcal{N}^{2m}a(v)+a(v)^{*}\mathcal{N}^{2m}\right)\leq C\mathcal{N}^{m}\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}+\mathcal{N}+1\right)\mathcal{N}^{m}.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
v^{\Lambda}
and
w^{\Lambda}
from Eq. (
2.15
) and Eq. (
2.16
), and that

\displaystyle v=v^{\Lambda}+\frac{1}{i}\nabla\cdot w^{\Lambda},

see Lemma
2.1
. Consequently, we can write the operator
a^{*}(v)
as

(7.5)

\displaystyle a^{*}(v)=a^{*}(v^{\Lambda})+[\mathcal{P},a^{*}(w^{\Lambda})].

Regarding the first term on the right side of Eq. (
7.5
) we use the fact that
\|v^{\Lambda}\|^{2}\lesssim\Lambda
, see Lemma
2.1
, in order to estimate for
\epsilon>0

(7.6)

\displaystyle\pm\left(a^{*}(v^{\Lambda})+a(v^{\Lambda})\right)\lesssim\epsilon\!\left(\mathcal{N}+\alpha^{-2}\right)+\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda\leq\epsilon\!\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)+\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda.

Furthermore, we use the fact that
\|w^{\Lambda}\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\Lambda}
, see Lemma
2.1
, in order to estimate for
\epsilon>0

(7.7)

\displaystyle\pm\left([\mathcal{P},a^{*}(w^{\Lambda})]+[\mathcal{P},a^{*}(w^{\Lambda})]^{*}\right)\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{P}^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda^{-1}\left(\mathcal{N}+\alpha^{-2}\right)\leq\epsilon\mathcal{P}^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda^{-1}\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right).

Combining what we have so far yields for a suitable constant
C>0
the operator inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{0}\!=\!\mathcal{P}^{2}\!+\!\mathcal{N}\!-\!a^{*}(v)\!-\!a(v)\!\geq\!(1\!-\!C\epsilon)\mathcal{P}^{2}\!+\!\left(1\!-\!C\epsilon\!-\!C\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda^{-1}\right)\!\mathcal{N}\!-\!C\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda^{-1}\!-\!C\epsilon\!-\!C\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda.

Choosing
\epsilon
small enough such that
C\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{4}
and
\Lambda
large enough such that
C\epsilon^{-1}\Lambda^{-1}\leq\frac{1}{4}
, yields for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{0}\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}-C,

which concludes the proof of Eq. (
7.1
). Eq. (
7.2
) can be verified analogously. Using Eq. (
7.5
) we can furthermore write

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{0}^{K}=\mathbb{H}+[\mathcal{P},a^{*}(w^{K})]+[\mathcal{P},a^{*}(w^{K})]^{*}\leq\mathbb{H}+CK^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}+\mathcal{N}+1\right),

where we have applied Eq. (
7.7
). Together with Eq. (
7.1
), this concludes the proof of Eq. (
7.3
). Regarding the proof of Eq. (
7.4
) we use again Eq. (
7.5
), and proceed similarly as in Eq. (
7.6
) and Eq. (
7.7
) for the choice
\Lambda:=1
, yielding

\displaystyle\pm\left(\mathcal{N}^{2m}a(v)+a(v)^{*}\mathcal{N}^{2m}\right)\leq C\mathcal{N}^{m}\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}+\mathcal{N}+1\right)\mathcal{N}^{m}.

∎

With Lemma
7.1
at hand, we are in a position to verify the following Lemma
7.2
.

Lemma 7.2
.

For all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C_{\ell}
, such that

\displaystyle\braket{\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\Psi_{\alpha}}_{\mathcal{F}}\leq C_{\ell},\ \ \ \ \braket{\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\mathcal{P}^{2}\Psi_{\alpha}}_{\mathcal{F}}\leq C_{\ell}.

Proof.

Let us first define for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
the quantities

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\ell,\alpha}:=\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}+\mathcal{N}\right)\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}.

Based on the operator inequality
\mathbb{H}_{0}-E_{\alpha}\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}-C
, see Lemma
7.1
together with the fact that
-c\leq E_{\alpha}\leq 0
for a suitable constant
c>0
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\ell,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\left(\mathbb{H}_{0}\!-\!E_{\alpha}\right)\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\!+\!C\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{2\ell}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},\left[\mathbb{H}_{0}\!-\!E_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\right]\right]\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\!+\!C\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{2\ell}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

(7.8)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2\alpha^{4}}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(\mathcal{N}^{2(\ell-1)}a(v)+a(v)^{*}\mathcal{N}^{2(\ell-1)}\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\!+\!C\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{2\ell}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}},

where we have used
\left(\mathbb{H}_{0}-E_{\alpha}\right)\Psi=0
and the fact that

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},\left[\mathbb{H}_{0}\!-\!E_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\right]\right]=\left[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},\left[a(v)^{*}+a(v),\mathcal{N}^{\ell}\right]\right]=-\frac{\ell^{2}}{\alpha^{4}}\left(\mathcal{N}^{2(\ell-1)}a(v)+a(v)^{*}\mathcal{N}^{2(\ell-1)}\right).

Combining Eq. (
7.4
) and Eq. (
7.8
) therefore yields for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\ell,\alpha}\leq C\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{\ell-1}\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}+\mathcal{N}\right)\mathcal{N}^{\ell-1}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+C\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{2(\ell-1)}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+C\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}^{2\ell}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}.

Clearly we have
C\mathcal{N}^{2(\ell-1)}+C\mathcal{N}^{2\ell}\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}^{2\ell+1}+\widetilde{C}
for a large enough constant
\widetilde{C}
, hence

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\ell,\alpha}\leq C\Gamma_{\ell-1,\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{\ell,\alpha}+\widetilde{C},

or equivalently
\Gamma_{\ell,\alpha}\leq 2C\Gamma_{\ell-1,\alpha}+2\widetilde{C}
. This concludes the proof by induction, since we have, using the operator inequality
\mathbb{H}_{0}-E_{\alpha}\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}-C
, that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0,\alpha}=\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}+\mathcal{N}\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq 2\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(\mathbb{H}-E_{\alpha}\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+2C=2C.

∎

In the following we want to verify that the probability measure
|\Psi_{\alpha}|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}
concentrates on sets of the form
\Omega_{\lambda}
as
\alpha\rightarrow\infty
, where
\Omega_{\lambda}
is defined in Eq. (
2.22
). For this purpose let us define for
\lambda>0
the probability
P_{\alpha}(\lambda)
of being outside of such a set

(7.9)

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(\lambda):=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\underset{\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{\lambda}^{(n)}}{\int}|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}Y_{n}.

In order to find good estimates on
P_{\alpha}(\lambda)
, we first need the subsequent auxiliary Lemma
7.3
and Lemma
7.4
. Lemma
7.3
provides control on the localization error with respect to the localization functions

\displaystyle F_{1,\lambda,u}(\rho):

\displaystyle=\chi_{u^{2}}\!\left(\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho-\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\underset{|x-y|>R_{*}}{\int\int}\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}\rho-\delta_{*}\right)^{2}\leq\lambda^{2}\right),

\displaystyle F_{2,\lambda,u}(\rho):

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-F_{1,\lambda,u}(\rho)^{2}},

as well as the localization functions

\displaystyle G_{1,D}(\rho):

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho\leq D\right),

\displaystyle G_{2,D}(\rho):

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-G_{1,D}(\rho)^{2}}.

Lemma 7.3
.

For
\lambda>0
and
u>0
there exists a constant
C>0
, such that

(7.10)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}+C\sqrt[4]{P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{\lambda-u}{2}\right)}\alpha^{-4}\leq E_{\alpha}+C\alpha^{-4}.

Furthermore, there exists a
C>0
, such that for any state
\Psi\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}
and
K\geq 0

(7.11)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle G_{j,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi,\mathbb{H}^{K}G_{j,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq\left\langle\Psi,\mathbb{H}^{K}\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}+CK^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{-4}.

Proof.

Using
\sum_{j=1}^{2}F_{j,\lambda,u}^{2}=1
and
[F_{j,\lambda,u},\mathcal{P}]=[F_{j,\lambda,u},\mathcal{N}]=0
, we observe that

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\Big\|\mathcal{P}F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\Big\|^{2},

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}.

In order to verify Eq. (
7.10
), it is therefore enough to verify

(7.12)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},a(v)^{*}F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},a(v)^{*}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\right|\leq C\sqrt[4]{P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{\lambda-u}{2}\right)}\alpha^{-4}\leq C\alpha^{-4}.

The proof of Eq. (
7.12
) is a consequence of the IMS identity for operators of the form
H(\rho_{Y_{n}})
, see
[
4
, Lemma 3.3]
and
[
16
, Theorem A.1]
, respectively
[
14
, Proposition 6.1]
, for the particle number operator
\mathcal{N}=\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}
specifically, which we will carry out in detail in the following. We compute

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})a(v)^{*}F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}-\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},a(v)^{*}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(\sum_{j=1}^{2}F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-1\right)L(v)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}=\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},F_{*}(Y_{n})L(v)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)},

with
F_{*}:\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined as

\displaystyle F_{*}(Y_{n}):=\sum_{j=1}^{2}F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-1=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n}})-F_{j,\lambda,u}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}.

Using the decomposition of
a(v)^{*}
for
\Lambda:=1
in Eq. (
7.5
) we can estimate

(7.13)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},F_{*}(Y_{n})L(v)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}=\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},F_{*}(Y_{n})L(v^{1})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},F_{*}(Y_{n})\mathcal{P}L(w^{1})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}-\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},F_{*}(Y_{n})L(w^{1})\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\left\|F_{*}(Y_{n})L(v^{1})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|+\left\|\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\left\|F_{*}(Y_{n})L(w^{1})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|+\left\|L(w^{1})^{*}F_{*}(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\left\|\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|,

where we have used that
\mathcal{P}
and
F_{*}
commute. Observe that
F_{*}(Y_{n})\neq 0
implies
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{\frac{\lambda-u}{\sqrt{2}}}
or
Y_{n-1}\notin\Omega_{\frac{\lambda-u}{\sqrt{2}}}
, and given that
\alpha
is large enough such that
(1+(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\lambda))\alpha^{-2}\leq u
we obtain in both cases
Y_{n-1}\notin\Omega_{\frac{\lambda-u}{2}}
. Using
\|h\|\lesssim 1
for
h\in\{v^{1},w^{1}\}
and
\|F_{*}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\alpha^{-4}
, see Eq. (
8.1
) in Section
8
, yields for
h\in\{v^{1},w^{1}\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \left\|F_{*}(Y_{n})L(u)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|^{2}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{n}{\alpha^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}F_{*}(Y_{n})^{2}|h(y_{n})|^{2}|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{-8}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{n}{\alpha^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{\frac{\lambda-u}{2}}^{(n)}}|h(y_{n})|^{2}|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}=\alpha^{-8}\|h\|^{2}\braket{\Psi_{\alpha},(\mathcal{N}+1)\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{\frac{\lambda-u}{2}}^{(n)}}(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{-8}\left\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\left\|\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{\frac{\lambda-u}{2}}^{(n)}}(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|=\alpha^{-8}\left\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\sqrt{P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{\lambda-u}{2}\right)}.

In a similar fashion we have
\left\|L(u)^{*}F_{*}(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|^{2}\lesssim\alpha^{-8}\left\|\mathcal{N}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\sqrt{P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{\lambda-u}{2}\right)}
. Together with the fact that
\|\mathcal{P}\Psi\|\lesssim 1
and
\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\lesssim 1
, see Lemma
7.2
, we obtain by Eq. (
7.13
)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},F_{*}(Y_{n})L(v)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|\lesssim\sqrt{\alpha^{-8}\sqrt{P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{\lambda-u}{2}\right)}}=\sqrt[4]{P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{\lambda-u}{2}\right)}\alpha^{-4}.

Since
P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{\lambda-u}{2}\right)
is a probability, i.e.
P_{\alpha}\!\left(\frac{\lambda-u}{2}\right)\leq 1
, this concludes the proof of Eq. (
7.12
), and therefore the proof of Eq. (
7.10
). Regarding Eq. (
7.11
), we proceed in a similar fashion as we did for Eq. (
7.10
) and estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle G_{j,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi,\mathbb{H}^{K}G_{j,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}-\left\langle\Psi,\mathbb{H}^{K}\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}

(7.14)

\displaystyle\ \ \leq\left\langle\Psi,G_{*}(\rho_{Y_{n}})L(v^{K})\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq\left\|G_{*}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi\right\|\left\|L(v^{K})\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi\right\|,

with
A:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\{Y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3n}:\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\leq D+1\}
and
G_{*}:\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined as

\displaystyle G_{*}(Y_{n}):=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[G_{j,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G_{j,D}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}.

Note that we have used the fact that
G_{*}(Y_{n})\neq 0
implies
Y_{n-1}\in A
in Eq. (
7.14
). Furthermore, we have
\|G_{*}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\alpha^{4}
, and therefore we can estimate the right hand side of Eq. (
7.14
) by

\displaystyle\left\|G_{*}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi\right\|\left\|L(v^{K})\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{-4}\left\|L(v^{K})\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi\right\|=\alpha^{-4}\|v^{K}\|\sqrt{\left\langle\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi,(\mathcal{N}+1)\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{-4}\|v^{K}\|\sqrt{D+2}\lesssim K^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{-4}.

∎

The following Lemma
7.4
shows that any state
\Psi\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}
supported outside of
\Omega_{\lambda}
, with
\lambda>0
, cannot have an energy close to the ground state energy
E_{\alpha}
. While the proof is essentially contained in
[
4
]
, we will work it out in detail here for the sake of completeness.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\Psi\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}
satisfy that
\Psi(x;Y_{n})\neq 0
implies
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{\lambda}
and define
K:=\alpha
. Then

(7.15)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi,\mathbb{H}^{K}\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}>E_{\alpha}+\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

Proof.

We are going to verify Eq. (
7.15
) by contradiction. Assume that Eq. (
7.15
) is violated

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi,\mathbb{H}^{K}\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}+\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

Then we obtain by Eq. (
7.11
) for
\alpha
large enough

(7.16)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle G_{j,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi,\mathbb{H}^{K}G_{j,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}+\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}+CK^{\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{-4}\leq E_{\alpha}+2\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}},

with the functions
G_{j,D}
defined above Lemma
7.3
. By Eq. (
7.2
) we immediately obtain for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\left\langle G_{2,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi,\mathbb{H}^{K}G_{2,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\langle G_{2,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi,(\mathcal{N}-C)G_{2,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}

(7.17)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \geq\frac{1}{2}(D-C)\left\|G_{2,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(D-C)\left(1-\left\|G_{1,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right\|^{2}\right),

where we have used the fact that
G_{2,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\neq 0
implies that
\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq D
, i.e. we have the operator inequality
\mathcal{N}\geq D
on the support of
G_{2,D}
. Let us define the state

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}:=\frac{1}{\left\|G_{1,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right\|}G_{1,D}(\rho_{Y_{n}}).

Choosing
D
large enough such that
\frac{1}{2}(D-C)\geq E_{\alpha}+2\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}
, we obtain by Eq. (
7.16
) and Eq. (
7.17
) the estimate

\displaystyle\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi},\mathbb{H}^{K}\widetilde{\Psi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}+2\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}\leq e^{\mathrm{Pek}}+2\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

Furthermore, we have
\mathcal{N}\leq D+1
on the support of
\widetilde{\Psi}
, and therefore
\widetilde{\Psi}
satisfies all assumptions of
[
4
, Theorem 3.2]
, which tells us that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C_{m}>0
and a (Borel) probability measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that

(7.18)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi},J(\rho_{Y_{n}})\widetilde{\Psi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}J(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{x})\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\right|\leq C_{m}\|j\|_{\infty}\alpha^{-\frac{2}{29}},

for any
J
of the form
J(\rho)=\int\dots\int j(z_{1},\dots,z_{m})\mathrm{d}\rho(z_{1})\dots\mathrm{d}\rho(z_{n})
with bounded and measurable
j:\mathbb{R}^{3m}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
, where
\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{x}(z):=\left|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(z-x)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}z
. Consider the concrete choice

\displaystyle J(\rho):=\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho-\int\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)^{2}+\left(\underset{|x-y|\leq R_{*}}{\int\int}\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}\rho-\delta_{*}\right)^{2},

which is a finite sum of admissible functions
J^{\prime}
in the sense described below Eq. (
7.18
). Furthermore, we have
J(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{x})=J(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{0})=0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi},J(\rho_{Y_{n}})\widetilde{\Psi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq C\alpha^{-\frac{2}{29}}

by Eq. (
7.18
). However, it follows immediately from the definition of
\Omega_{\lambda}
that
J(\rho_{Y_{n}})\geq\lambda^{2}
for
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{\lambda}
. Together with the assumption that
\Psi(x;Y_{n})\neq 0
implies
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{\lambda}
, this yields for
\alpha
large enough such that
C\alpha^{-\frac{2}{29}}<\lambda^{2}
the desired contradiction

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}\leq\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi},J(\rho_{Y_{n}})\widetilde{\Psi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq C\alpha^{-\frac{2}{29}}<\lambda^{2}.

∎

The following Lemma
7.5
shows that the measure
|\Psi_{\alpha}|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}
is supported on the set
\Omega_{\lambda}
, up to a probability of the size
\alpha^{-5}
. While the estimate by
\alpha^{-5}
is sufficient for our purpose, it is possible to improve this to
\alpha^{-m}
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
with some additional effort.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\lambda>0
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(\lambda)\leq C\alpha^{-5}.

Proof.

Trivially, we have
P_{\alpha}(\lambda)\leq 1
for all
\lambda\geq 0
, and therefore we are done by iteration, once we can verify that for all
\lambda>0
and
u>0
there exists a constant
C
such that

(7.19)

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(2(\lambda+u))\leq C\sqrt[4]{P_{\alpha}(\lambda)}\alpha^{-4\left(1-\frac{1}{29}\right)}.

To be more precise, Eq. (
7.19
) implies for all
r<\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{4}}4\left(1-\frac{1}{29}\right)
, such as
r:=5
, and
\lambda>0
that there exists a constant
C_{r,\lambda}
such that

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(\lambda)\leq C_{r,\lambda}\alpha^{-r}.

Let us define the functions
F_{j}:=F_{j,2\lambda+u,u}
, introduced above Lemma
7.3
. By Lemma
7.3
we obtain for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle F_{j}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}F_{j}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+C\sqrt[4]{P_{\alpha}(\lambda)}\alpha^{-4}=E_{\alpha}+C\sqrt[4]{P_{\alpha}(\lambda)}\alpha^{-4}.

In combination with the operator inequality
\mathbb{H}_{0}\geq E_{\alpha}
, and the fact that

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(2(\lambda+u))\leq\|F_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}=1-\|F_{1}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2},

we obtain for the state

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}:=\frac{1}{\|F_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|}F_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}

the estimate

(7.20)

\displaystyle P_{\alpha}(2(\lambda+u))\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},(\mathbb{H}_{0}-E_{\alpha})\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq\|F_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},(\mathbb{H}_{0}-E_{\alpha})\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq C\sqrt[4]{P_{\alpha}(\lambda)}\alpha^{-4}.

In the following we want to show by contradiction that

(7.21)

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\geq E_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{3}\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}},

i.e. we assume

(7.22)

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}<E_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{3}\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

By Eq. (
7.3
) we therefore obtain for
K:=\alpha
and
\alpha
large enough

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}_{0}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\leq E_{\alpha}+\frac{2}{3}\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

Let us furthermore define the auxiliary state
\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}\subseteq L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)
as

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n}):=\tau_{\epsilon}(x)\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}(y_{1}-x,\dots,y_{n}-x),

where
\tau_{\epsilon}(x):=\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\tau\!\left(\epsilon x\right)
and
\tau
is a
[0,1]
-valued smooth function with compact support and
\int\tau^{2}\mathrm{d}x=1
. Note that there exists a constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that for
\epsilon>0

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left\langle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha},(-\Delta_{x})\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}=\left\langle\tau_{\epsilon}\otimes\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\left(\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}-\mathcal{P}\right)^{2}\tau_{\epsilon}\otimes\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\left(1+\epsilon^{-1}\right)\!\left\langle\tau_{\epsilon},(-\Delta_{x})\tau_{\epsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)}\leq\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathcal{P}^{2}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+C^{\prime}\!\left(\epsilon+\epsilon^{2}\right),

where we have used the assumption in Eq. (
7.22
) and the fact that
\mathcal{P}^{2}\leq 2\mathbb{H}_{0}+C
for a suitable constant
C>0
, see Eq. (
7.1
). Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left\langle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha},\Big\{\mathcal{N}-a^{*}(v^{\Lambda}_{x})-a(v^{\Lambda}_{x})\Big\}\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}=\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\Big\{\mathcal{N}-a^{*}(v^{\Lambda})-a(v^{\Lambda})\Big\}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}.

Therefore we obtain for
\epsilon:=\alpha^{-1}
and
\alpha
large enough

(7.23)

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq\left\langle\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}_{0}\widehat{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+C^{\prime}\alpha^{-1}+C^{\prime}\alpha^{-2}\leq E_{\alpha}+\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

It follows however from Lemma
7.4
, that any state satisfying Eq. (
7.23
) cannot be supported outside of
\Omega_{\lambda}
for
\lambda>0
and
\alpha
large enough. Since the state
\widehat{\Phi}_{\alpha}
is supported on the set
\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)\setminus\Omega_{\sqrt{2}\lambda}
, this is the desired contradiction to Eq. (
7.22
). Combining Eq. (
7.20
) and Eq. (
7.21
) concludes the proof of Eq. (
7.19
).
∎

8.
Analysis of the Error Terms
\mathcal{E}

It is the goal of this Section, to show that the residual terms
\mathcal{E}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{4}
appearing in the analysis of the quantum energy

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha,p},\mathbb{H}_{p}\Psi_{\alpha,p}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}

in Subsection
3.2
are indeed small compared to
\alpha^{-4}|p|^{2}
, where we rely heavily on the results of the previous Sections
6
and
7
. As a first step let us verify the following auxiliary Lemma
8.1
.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be as in the definition of
\mathcal{B}
in Eq. (
3.5
). Then there exist constants
C,d>0
, such that for all
Y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3n}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}

(8.1)

\displaystyle\left|F(\rho_{Y_{n}})-F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|\leq\frac{C}{\alpha^{2}},\ \ \ \ \ \left|\sqrt{1-F(\rho_{Y_{n}})^{2}}-\sqrt{1-F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{C}{\alpha^{2}},

and in the case that
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
for all
i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|

(8.2)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\left(\|f\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)}{\alpha^{2}}\left(1+\min\{|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}})|,|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\}\right)\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\geq d\right),

and

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|G_{i}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\mathcal{B}_{j}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!G_{i}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\mathcal{B}_{j}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|

(8.3)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)}{\alpha^{2}}\!\left(1+\min\{|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}})|,|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\}\right)^{2}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\geq d\right).

Furthermore,
0\leq\varphi_{\eta}(Y_{n})\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{4}\alpha^{4}}
for
\eta\leq q
and
Y_{n}
satisfying
G(\rho_{Y_{n}})\neq G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})
or
F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\neq 0
, and
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon
be small enough such that
\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa+\epsilon<\frac{(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}-\sigma-\kappa-\epsilon)^{2}}{2}
and let
\alpha
be large enough such that
(1+2(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa))\alpha^{-2}\leq\epsilon
. Note that
F(\rho_{Y_{n}})-F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\neq 0
implies that
\rho_{Y_{n-1}}
or
\rho_{Y_{n}}
is an element of
\Omega_{\sigma+\kappa}
. In both cases we obtain

(8.4)

\displaystyle d:=\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}-\sigma-\kappa-\epsilon\leq\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\leq\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa+\epsilon,\ \ \ \underset{|x-y|>R}{\int\int}\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\nu\leq\delta_{*}+\sigma+\kappa+\epsilon.

Since the function
\chi_{\kappa^{2}}(\cdot\leq\delta^{2})
, as well as
\sqrt{1-\chi_{\kappa^{2}}(\cdot\leq\delta^{2})^{2}}
, in the definition of
F
in Eq. (
3.6
) is smooth, Eq. (
8.1
) follows immediately from the observation that

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}-\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}=\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}},

\displaystyle 0\leq\underset{|x-y|>R}{\int\int}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}-\underset{|x-y|>R}{\int\int}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}=\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}}\int_{|x-y_{n}|}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\leq\frac{2(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa+\epsilon)}{\alpha^{2}}.

Regarding the proof of Eq. (
8.2
), we first note that Eq. (
8.4
) holds as well in case

\displaystyle G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\neq 0,

and therefore we have for all such
Y_{n}
that

(8.5)

\displaystyle\left|m_{q_{*}}\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)-m_{q_{*}}\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C}{\alpha^{2}q_{*}}

by
[
4
, Lemma 3.10]
for a suitable constant
C>0
and
q_{*}\leq q
, and furthermore we have by
[
4
, Lemma 3.9]
that the quantiles in the definition of
H^{\chi}_{q,\rho_{Y_{n-1}}}
in Eq. (
5.7
) satisfy

(8.6)

\displaystyle\left|x^{+}_{j,q}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-x^{-}_{j,q}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|\leq 2R_{*}.

Especially we see that
(\rho_{Y_{n-1}},\rho_{Y_{n-1}},\rho_{Y_{n}})
is an admissible triple in the sense of Lemma
5.2
, and therefore we obtain for a suitable constant
C>0

(8.7)

\displaystyle\left|m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)\!-\!m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right)\right|\leq\|g*(\rho_{Y_{n}}\!-\!\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\!+\!\left|\int H^{\chi}_{q,\rho_{Y_{n-1}}}\mathrm{d}(\rho_{Y_{n}}\!-\!\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|\leq\frac{C}{\alpha^{2}},

where we have used
\|g*(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\|\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}
and
\|H^{\chi}_{q,\rho_{Y_{n-1}}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
, which is a consequence of Eq. (
8.6
). We compute

(8.8)

\displaystyle\ G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})=\left[F(\rho_{Y_{n}})-F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\!\left[m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)-m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\alpha^{-2}F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})f\!\left(y_{n}\!-\!m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)\right)

(8.9)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\!F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\int\left[f(y\!-\!m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}}\right))\!-\!f(y\!-\!m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right))\right]\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}.

Using Eq. (
8.1
) and Eq. (
8.7
), as well as

\displaystyle\ \ \ \left|\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|\leq\left|m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)\right|+\|f\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{Y_{n}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}},

\displaystyle\|\rho_{Y_{n}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\|\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\alpha^{-2}\leq\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa+\epsilon

and

\displaystyle\int\left[f(y\!-\!m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}}\right))\!-\!f(y\!-\!m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right))\right]\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}

\displaystyle\ \ \leq\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\left|m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)\!-\!m_{q}\!\left(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right)\right|,

concludes the proof of Eq. (
8.2
). Eq. (
8.3
) can be verified analogously. Regarding the estimate on
\varphi_{\eta}
, let us use the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tau_{\eta}(y)\mathrm{d}y=1
in order to compute

\displaystyle\varphi_{\eta}(Y_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left[\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})+x\right)-\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x\right)\right]^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\nabla\tau_{\eta}\|^{2}}{2}\left|m_{\eta}\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n}}\right)-m_{\eta}\!\left(\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\right)\right|^{2}\leq C\eta^{-4}\alpha^{-4},

for a suitable
C>0
, where we have used Eq. (
8.5
) and the fact that
\|\nabla\tau_{\eta}\|=\eta^{-1}\|\nabla\tau\|
.
∎

With Lemma
8.1
at hand we are going to verify that the expressions
\mathcal{E}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{4}
can be considered as being small in magnitude. It will be the content of the following Lemma
8.2
to establish estimates on

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}=\frac{1}{i}\Big\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\xi_{p}(Y_{n})L(\nabla\cdot w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)},

introduced in Eq. (
3.19
).

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be as in the definition of
\mathcal{B}
in Eq. (
3.5
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for
\Lambda>0

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\leq C\left(1+\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\frac{|p|^{2}}{\sqrt{\Lambda}\alpha^{4}}.

Proof.

We start with the simple identity

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{i}\Big\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\xi_{p}(Y_{n})L(\nabla\cdot w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}=\Big\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\xi_{p}(Y_{n})\big[\mathcal{P},L(w^{\Lambda})\big]\Psi_{\alpha}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

(8.10)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha},\xi_{p}L(w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}-\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\xi_{p}L(w^{\Lambda})\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},[\xi_{p},\mathcal{P}]L(w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}.

Regarding the first term on the right hand side of Eq. (
8.10
) we use that
\|\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\|\lesssim 1
, see Lemma
7.2
, and estimate

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha},\xi_{p}L(w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|^{2}\leq\|\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}\,\|\xi_{p}L(w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}\lesssim\|\xi_{p}L(w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\ \ =\sum_{n=1}^{D_{\alpha}}\frac{n}{\alpha^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\left|w^{\Lambda}(y_{n})\xi_{p}(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n},

where we only consider
n\leq D_{\alpha}:=(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa)\alpha^{2}+1
, since

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp}\!\left(\xi_{p}\right)\cap\mathbb{R}^{3n}\subseteq\Omega^{(n)}_{\sigma+\kappa}\cup\Omega^{(n-1)}_{\sigma+\kappa}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}=\emptyset

in case
n>D_{\alpha}
. By Eq. (
8.2
) we furthermore have

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left|\xi_{p}(Y_{n})\right|\!=\!\Big|p\!\cdot\!\left[G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]\Big|^{2}\!\left|\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{0}^{t}\!e^{isp\cdot\left[G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}t\right|

(8.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\frac{|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{4}}\Big(1\!+\!\min\{|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}})|,|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\}\Big)^{2}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\geq d\right),

for any
Y_{n}
satisfying
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
. Consequently

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \frac{1}{\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}}\left|\left\langle\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha},\xi_{p}L(w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}|w^{\Lambda}(y_{n})|^{2}\Big(1+|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}})|\Big)^{4}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\geq d\right)\frac{|p|^{4}}{\alpha^{8}}\left|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{4}}\|w^{\Lambda}\|\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(1+|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\right)^{4}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{|p|^{2}}{\sqrt{\Lambda}\alpha^{4}},

where we have used that
\|w^{\Lambda}\|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}
, see Lemma
2.1
. In order to estimate the second term on the right hand side of Eq. (
8.10
) we use again
\|\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\|\lesssim 1
and compute

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \left|\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\xi_{p}L(w^{\Lambda})\mathcal{P}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|^{2}\lesssim\|L(w^{\Lambda})^{*}\xi_{p}^{*}\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{D_{\alpha}}\frac{n}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3(n-1)}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{\Lambda}(y_{n})\xi^{*}_{p}(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})\mathrm{d}y_{n}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{\Lambda}\|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3(n-1)}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|\xi^{*}_{p}(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}y_{n}\mathrm{d}Y_{n-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\frac{|p|^{4}}{\Lambda\alpha^{8}}\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(1+|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\right)^{4}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\frac{|p|^{4}}{\Lambda\alpha^{8}},

where we have used Eq. (
8.11
) and Theorem
6.1
again in the last estimate. Regarding the third term on the right hand side of Eq. (
8.10
), we proceed in a similar fashion as we did for the first term and estimate

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},[\xi_{p},\mathcal{P}]L(w^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|\lesssim\|w^{\Lambda}\|\left\|[\xi_{p},\mathcal{P}]^{*}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\left\|[\xi_{p},\mathcal{P}]^{*}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|.

An explicit computation reveals that
[\xi_{p},\mathcal{P}]
is a multiplication operator given by

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ [\xi_{p},\mathcal{P}](Y_{n})=i\sum_{j=1}^{n}\partial_{y_{j}}\xi_{p}(Y_{n})

\displaystyle=-ip\cdot\left[G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right]\int_{0}^{1}\!e^{itp\cdot\left[G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right]}\mathrm{d}t\left(F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right)p.

Using Eq. (
8.11
) and
\left|F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})-F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}
, see Eq. (
8.1
), and Theorem
6.1
yields

\displaystyle\left\|[\xi_{p},\mathcal{P}]^{*}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)\frac{|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{4}}\left\|\left(1+|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\right)\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)\frac{|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{4}}.

∎

In the subsequent Lemma
8.3
we will analyse the residual terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\!\Psi_{\alpha},\left(\xi_{p}(Y_{n})+\frac{1}{2}\left[p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\right)L(v^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\!\Big\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle\!\Psi_{\alpha},\!\varphi_{\eta}(Y_{n})\left[p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\!L(v^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\!\Big\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

introduced in Eq. (
3.23
) and Eq. (
3.30
) respectively.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be as in the definition of
\mathcal{B}
in Eq. (
3.5
) and
\eta=\alpha^{-\beta}
as in Eq. (
3.25
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for
\Lambda>0
and
\eta\leq q

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{2}|\leq\frac{C\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{3}\sqrt{\Lambda}|p|^{3}}{\alpha^{6}},\ \ \ \ \ |\mathcal{E}_{3}|\leq\frac{C\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\sqrt{\Lambda}|p|^{2}}{\eta^{4}\alpha^{8}}.

Proof.

Regarding the term
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, note that we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp}\!\left(\xi_{p}(Y_{n})+\frac{1}{2}\left[p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\right)\cap\mathbb{R}^{3n}\subseteq\Omega^{(n)}_{\sigma+\kappa}\cup\Omega^{(n-1)}_{\sigma+\kappa}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}=:A^{n},

and therefore

(8.12)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{2}|\leq 2\|L(v^{\Lambda})\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi_{\alpha}\|\,\|\!\left(\xi_{p}+\frac{1}{2}\left[p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\right)\!\Psi_{\alpha}\|.

Using the fact that
A^{n}=\emptyset
in case
n>D_{\alpha}:=(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa)\alpha^{2}+1
, we can easily estimate the first factor on the right hand side of Eq. (
8.12
) by

\displaystyle\|L(v^{\Lambda})\mathds{1}_{A}\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}\!\leq\!\sum_{n=1}^{D_{\alpha}}\!\!\frac{n}{\alpha^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\!\!\!\!\left|v^{\Lambda}(y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\!\lesssim\|v^{\Lambda}\|^{2}\!\lesssim\!\Lambda,

where we have used that
\|v^{\Lambda}\|^{2}\lesssim\Lambda
, see Lemma
2.1
. In order to estimate the second factor on the right hand side of Eq. (
8.12
), note that we have by Eq. (
8.2
)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|\xi_{p}(Y_{n})+\frac{1}{2}\left[p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\big|p\!\cdot\!\left[G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]\big|^{3}\left|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}e^{irp\cdot\left[G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq|p|^{3}\left|G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|^{3}\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{3}\Big(1+|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\Big)^{3}\frac{|p|^{3}}{\alpha^{6}}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d\right)

for all
Y_{n}
with
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
. Consequently, we obtain using Theorem
6.1

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ |\mathcal{E}_{2}|\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\|\!\left(\xi_{p}+\frac{1}{2}\left[p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\right)\!\Psi_{\alpha}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{3}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}|p|^{3}}{\alpha^{6}}\left\|\Big(1+|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\Big)^{3}\Psi_{\alpha}\right\|\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{3}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}|p|^{3}}{\alpha^{6}}.

Utilizing the fact that
\|\mathds{1}_{\widetilde{A}}\varphi_{\eta}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta^{4}\alpha^{4}}
, where
\widetilde{A}
is the set of all
Y_{n}
such that
G(\rho_{Y_{n}})\neq G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})
, see Lemma
8.1
, we proceed similarly by

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{3}|\lesssim\|v^{\Lambda}\|\|\mathds{1}_{\widetilde{A}}\varphi_{\eta}\|_{\infty}\|\left[p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})-p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right]^{2}\Psi_{\alpha}\|\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\sqrt{\Lambda}\frac{1}{\eta^{4}\alpha^{4}}\frac{|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{4}}.

∎

Finally, we are going to derive estimates on the residual term

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mathcal{E}_{4}=-\frac{1}{2}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\!\!\Big\langle\!\tau_{\eta}(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!+\!x)\Psi_{\alpha},\!\left(F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right)\!L(v^{\Lambda})\tau_{\eta}(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!+\!x)\,\Psi_{\alpha}\!\Big\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\!\mathrm{d}x

introduced in Eq. (
3.31
).

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be as in the definition of
\mathcal{B}
in Eq. (
3.5
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{4}|\leq\frac{C\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)\sqrt{\Lambda}|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{6}}.

Proof.

Let us introduce the function
c(Y_{n})
as

\displaystyle c(Y_{n}):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x\right)\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})+x\right)\mathrm{d}x,

which allows us to write

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mathcal{E}_{4}=-\Big\langle\Psi_{\alpha},c(Y_{n})\!\left(F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right)\!L(v^{\Lambda})\Psi_{\alpha}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\!.

Note that
|c(Y_{n})|\leq\frac{1}{2}
for all
Y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and by Eq. (
8.3
) we obtain for
y_{i}\neq y_{j}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \left|\left(p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n}})p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!p\!\cdot\!G(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})p\!\cdot\!\mathcal{B}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)}{\alpha^{2}}|p|^{2}\!\left(1+|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n-1}})|\right)^{2}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\geq d\right)=:\frac{C|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\zeta(Y_{n-1}).

Consequently,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{4}|\!

\displaystyle\lesssim\!\frac{|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\!\!\sum_{n=1}^{D_{\alpha}}\!\!\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\alpha}\!\!\underset{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}{\int}\!\left|F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|\zeta(Y_{n-1})|v^{\Lambda}(y_{n})|\left|\overline{\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})}\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})\right|\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\!\frac{|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\!\sum_{n=1}^{D_{\alpha}}\!\underset{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}{\int}\!\!\!\left|F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|\zeta(Y_{n-1})|v^{\Lambda}(y_{n})|\left|\overline{\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})}\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})\right|\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

(8.13)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|p|^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\|v^{\Lambda}\|\,\|\zeta(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}\|\,\|\!\left(F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right)\!\Psi_{\alpha}\|,

where we only consider
n\leq D_{\alpha}:=(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa)\alpha^{2}+1
, since

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp}\!\left(F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right)\cap\mathbb{R}^{3n}

\displaystyle\subseteq\Omega^{(n)}_{\sigma+\kappa}\cup\Omega^{(n-1)}_{\sigma+\kappa}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}=\emptyset

in case
n>D_{\alpha}
. Making use of Theorem
6.1
we furthermore obtain

\displaystyle\|\zeta(Y_{n})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\left(1\!+\!|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\right)^{4}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n-1}}\geq d\right)|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\left(1\!+\!|m_{q}(\chi*\rho_{Y_{n}})|\right)^{4}\chi\!\left(\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\geq d\right)|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|f\|_{\infty}\!+\!\|\nabla f\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}.

Finally, we note that
\left|F(\rho_{Y_{n}})-F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}
by Eq. (
8.1
) and
F(\rho_{Y_{n}})-F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\neq 0
implies
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{\frac{\sigma-\kappa}{\sqrt{2}}}
or
Y_{n-1}\notin\Omega_{\frac{\sigma-\kappa}{\sqrt{2}}}
. In both cases we have
Y_{n}\notin\Omega_{\frac{\sigma-\kappa}{\sqrt{2}}-\alpha^{-2}}\subseteq\Omega_{\frac{\sigma-\kappa}{\sqrt{2}}-\epsilon}
for
\epsilon>0
and
\alpha
large enough and therefore

\displaystyle\|\!\left(F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\!-\!F(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})\right)\!\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}\lesssim\alpha^{-4}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\underset{\mathbb{R}^{3n}\setminus\Omega_{\frac{\sigma-\kappa}{\sqrt{2}}-\epsilon}^{(n)}}{\int}\!\!\!|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\!\lesssim\!\alpha^{-8},

for
\epsilon
small enough, see Lemma
7.5
. This concludes the proof by Eq. (
8.13
), together with the fact that
\|v^{\Lambda}\|\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}
, see Lemma
2.1
.
∎

9.
Bose-Einstein Condensation

In the following we want to establish that the state
\Phi_{\alpha}
, defined in Eq. (
3.27
)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n})=\mu_{\alpha}^{-1}\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right)F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}(y_{1}-x_{1},\dots,y_{n}-x),

satisfies Bose-Einstein condensation, and as a consequence we are going to show that the typical empirical measures
\rho_{Y_{n}}
of the phonon positions
Y_{n}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{n})
are close to the Pekar measure
\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}=\rho_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}
with respect to the mollified total variation. As a first step, we are going to show in the subsequent Lemma
9.1
that the state
\Phi_{\alpha}
defined in Eq. (
3.27
) is a low energy state of the cut-off Hamiltonian
\mathbb{H}^{K}
, or equivalently that
\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{T}^{*}\Phi_{\alpha}
, defined in Eq. (
3.26
)

\displaystyle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n})=\mu_{\alpha}^{-1}\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x\right)F(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n}),

is a low energy state of
\mathbb{H}^{K}_{\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}}
.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
K:=\alpha^{s}
with
\frac{8}{29}<s\leq 1
. Then we have

(9.1)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\leq E_{\alpha}+\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

Proof.

As a first step we note that
\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{T}^{*}\Phi_{\alpha}
and
\mathcal{T}^{*}\mathbb{H}^{K}\mathcal{T}=\mathbb{H}^{K}_{\frac{1}{i}\nabla}
, and therefore

(9.2)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}=\left\langle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}_{\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}}\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}.

Using the localization functions
F_{1}:=F
and
F_{2}:=\sqrt{1-F^{2}}
, we obtain by Lemma
7.3
for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\langle F_{j}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}F_{j}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle\leq E_{\alpha}+C\alpha^{-4}.

Since
\mathbb{H}_{0}\geq E_{\alpha}
as an operator inequality, we obtain for the state

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}:=\mu_{\alpha}^{-1}F_{1}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha},

with
\mu_{\alpha}=\|F_{1}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|=\sqrt{1-\|F_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}}
introduced below Eq. (
3.26
), the estimate

\displaystyle\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}_{0}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle\leq\mu_{\alpha}^{-2}\left(E_{\alpha}+C\alpha^{-4}-E_{\alpha}\|F_{2}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\Psi_{\alpha}\|^{2}\right)=E_{\alpha}+\frac{C\alpha^{-4}}{\mu_{\alpha}^{2}}\leq E_{\alpha}+2C\alpha^{-4},

where we have used that
\mu_{\alpha}^{2}=1-\left\langle\Psi_{\alpha},\left(1-F(\rho_{Y_{n}})^{2}\right)\Psi_{\alpha}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1}{2}
by Lemma
7.5
. Together with Eq. (
7.3
) we obtain for a suitable constant
C>0
and
\alpha
large enough

(9.3)

\displaystyle\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}_{0}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle\leq E_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

In order to compute the term on the right hand side of Eq. (
9.2
), let us first write

\displaystyle\left\langle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}_{\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}}\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}=\Big\|\Big(\mathcal{P}-\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}\Big)\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\Big\|^{2}+\left\langle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}

(9.4)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -2\mathfrak{Re}\left\langle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha},L(v^{K})\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}.

We note that
\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n})=\tau_{\eta}(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x)\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}(Y_{n})
, and that the operator
\tau_{\eta}(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x)
commutes with
\mathcal{P}-\frac{1}{i}\nabla
, and therefore

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\mathcal{P}-\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}\Big)\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\Big\|^{2}=\Big\|\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x\right)\Big(\mathcal{P}-\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}\Big)\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\Big\|^{2}=\Big\|\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x\right)\mathcal{P}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\ \ =\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)|\mathcal{P}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

(9.5)

\displaystyle\ \ =\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}|\mathcal{P}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}(Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}Y_{n}=\Big\|\mathcal{P}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\Big\|^{2}.

In a similar fashion we have

(9.6)

\displaystyle\left\langle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}=\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}.

Regarding
\left\langle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha},L(v^{K})\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}
, recall the definition of
\varphi_{\eta}
in Eq. (
3.29
)

\displaystyle\varphi_{\eta}(Y_{n})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left[\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})+x\right)-\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})+x\right)\right]\tau_{\eta}\!\left(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n-1}})+x\right)\mathrm{d}x,

and use the fact that
0\leq\varphi_{\eta}(Y_{n})\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{4}}\alpha^{-4}
for a suitable constant
C>0
and
Y_{n}
in the support of
\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}
, see Lemma
8.1
, in order to obtain

(9.7)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|\left\langle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha},L(v^{K})\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}-\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha},L(v^{K})\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\left|\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha},\varphi_{\eta}(Y_{n})L(v^{K})\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{4}}\alpha^{-4}\!\!\!\sum_{n=1}^{(\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa)\alpha^{2}}\!\!\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\left|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n})\right|\left|\Psi_{\alpha}(Y_{n-1})\right|\left|v^{K}(y_{n})\right|\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\lesssim\eta^{-4}\alpha^{-4}\|v^{K}\|\lesssim\eta^{-4}\alpha^{-4}\sqrt{K}.

By our choice of
\eta
in Eq. (
3.25
) and the assumption
K\leq\alpha
, we obtain

\displaystyle\eta^{-4}\alpha^{-4}\sqrt{K}\leq\frac{1}{4}\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}

for a suitable constant
C>0
and
\alpha
large enough. Combining Eq. (
9.4
), Eq. (
9.5
), Eq. (
9.6
) and Eq. (
9.7
) yields

\displaystyle\left\langle\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}_{\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x}}\overline{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\!\!\!L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\Big\|\mathcal{P}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\Big\|^{2}\!\!\!+\!\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}\!\!\!-\!2\mathfrak{Re}\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha},L(v^{K})\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\!\!\!L^{2}\!\left(\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\!\!+\!\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}_{0}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{F}}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

This concludes the proof by Eq. (
9.3
).
∎

The following result, concerning the Weyl operator
W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}
with respect to the state
\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
, see Eq. (
2.10
), is a direct consequence of Lemma
9.1
and
[
4
, Lemma 3.11]
, and establishes that
\Phi_{\alpha}
satisfies Bose-Einstein condensation.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
\Phi_{\alpha}
be defined in Eq. (
3.27
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},W^{-1}_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\mathcal{N}W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq C\alpha^{-\frac{2}{29}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.1
, together with the trivial observation that
E_{\alpha}\leq e^{\mathrm{Pek}}
, we have for
K:=\alpha

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)}\leq E_{\alpha}+\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}\leq e^{\mathrm{Pek}}+\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}},

and furthermore note that
\mathrm{supp}(\Phi_{\alpha})\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\Big(\mathrm{supp}\big(\tau_{\eta}(m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}}))\big)\cap\mathrm{supp}\big(F\big)\Big)
, which especially implies that for all
Y_{n}\in\mathrm{supp}(\Phi_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}\leq\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\sigma+\kappa,\ \ \ \left|m_{\eta}(\rho_{Y_{n}})\right|\leq\eta.

Therefore,
\Phi_{\alpha}
satisfies the assumptions of
[
4
, Lemma 3.11]
with corresponding constants
q:=\epsilon:=\alpha^{-\beta}\leq\alpha^{-\frac{2}{29}}
, see the definition of
\eta
in Eq. (
3.25
), which concludes the proof.
∎

With Lemma
9.2
at hand, we are in a position to verify that a typical empirical measure
\rho_{Y_{n}}
is expected to be close to the Pekar measure
\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}
with respect to the mollified total variation.

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}
be the Pekar measure
\mathrm{d}\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}:=|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x
and let
\Phi_{\alpha}
be defined in Eq. (
3.27
). Then there exists an
\epsilon>0
such that for
T\geq 1

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\!\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\Phi_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)\!\mathrm{d}Y_{n}\leq\alpha^{-\epsilon}T^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Proof.

Let us introduce for
R>0
the notation

\displaystyle\varphi_{R}:

\displaystyle=\chi(|\cdot|\leq R)\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\rho}_{R}:

\displaystyle=\left|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{R}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle g_{T,x}(y):

\displaystyle=g_{T}(y-x),

and express the total variation between the convoluted measures as

(9.8)

\displaystyle\|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\widetilde{\rho}_{R})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|\int g_{T,x}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}-\int g_{T,x}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\rho}_{R}\right|\mathrm{d}x.

Using an orthonormal basis
\{u_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
and the notation

\displaystyle G_{n,m}(x):

\displaystyle=\braket{u_{m},g_{T,x}u_{n}},

\displaystyle\varphi_{R,n}:

\displaystyle=\braket{u_{n},\varphi_{R}},

\displaystyle b_{n}:

\displaystyle=a(u_{n})-\varphi_{R,n},

we can express by Eq. (
2.4
) for fixed
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
the multiplication operator by
Y_{n}\mapsto\int g_{T,x}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}
, acting on the Hilbert space
L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigcup}\mathbb{R}^{3n}\right)
, as

\displaystyle\int g_{T,x}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}-\int g_{T,x}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\rho}_{R}=\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)\left(a_{n}^{*}a_{m}-\overline{\varphi}_{R,n}\varphi_{R,m}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)\left(b_{n}^{*}b_{m}+b_{n}^{*}\varphi_{R,m}+\overline{\varphi}_{R,n}b_{m}\right).

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we therefore obtain

\displaystyle\left(\int g_{x}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}-\int g_{x}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\rho}_{R}\right)^{2}\leq 3\big(A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}\big),

with
A_{1}:=\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}b_{m}\right)^{2}
and

\displaystyle A_{2}:

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}\varphi_{R,m}\right)\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)\overline{\varphi}_{R,n}b_{m}\right),

\displaystyle A_{3}:

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)\overline{\varphi}_{R,n}b_{m}\right)\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}\varphi_{R,m}\right).

Using the canonical commutation relations
[b_{m},b_{n}^{*}]=\alpha^{-2}\delta_{m,n}
we obtain

\displaystyle A_{3}=A_{2}+\alpha^{-2}\|g_{T,x}\varphi_{R}\|^{2}.

Furthermore, let
G^{\prime}(x):L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)\longrightarrow L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
be the rank one operator, defined in coordinates as
G^{\prime}_{n,m}(x)=\braket{u_{n},g_{x}\varphi_{R}}\overline{\braket{u_{m},g_{x}\varphi_{R}}}
. Clearly

\displaystyle\|G^{\prime}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|g_{T,x}\varphi_{R}\|^{2},

and therefore

\displaystyle A_{2}=\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G^{\prime}_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}b_{m}\leq\|g_{T,x}\varphi_{R}\|^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}.

In the following let
r>0
be large enough such that
\mathrm{supp}(g)\subseteq B_{r}(0)
, and note that
g_{x}\varphi_{R}=0
in case
|x|>R+r
. Summarizing what we have so far yields for
\lambda>0

\displaystyle\left(\int g_{T,x}\mathrm{d}\rho_{Y_{n}}-\int g_{T,x}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\rho}_{R}\right)^{2}\leq 3\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}b_{m}\right)^{2}+3\|g_{T,x}\varphi_{R}\|^{2}\left(2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}+\alpha^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \leq 3\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}b_{m}\right)^{2}+3\lambda\|g_{T,x}\varphi_{R}\|^{4}+\chi(|x|\!\leq\!R\!+\!r)3\lambda^{-1}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}+\alpha^{-2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \leq 3\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}b_{m}+\lambda\|g_{T,x}\varphi_{R}\|^{2}+\lambda^{-1}\chi(|x|\!\leq\!R\!+\!r)\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}+\alpha^{-2}\right)\right)^{2},

where we have used in the last estimate that all the components
\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}b_{m}
,
\|g_{T,x}\varphi_{R}\|^{2}
and
\chi(|x|\!\leq\!R\!+\!r)\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}+1\right)
are non-negative and commute. Since the square root
t\mapsto\sqrt{t}
is operator monotone, we therefore have by Eq. (
9.8
)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \|g_{T}\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho}_{R})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{3}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\!\left(\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}G_{n,m}(x)b_{n}^{*}b_{m}\!+\!\lambda\|g_{T,x}\varphi_{R}\|^{2}\!+\!\lambda^{-1}\chi(|x|\!\leq\!R\!+\!r)\!\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}\!+\!\alpha^{-2}\right)\!\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\!\sqrt{3}\!\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}\!\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G_{n,m}(x)\mathrm{d}x\right)b_{n}^{*}b_{m}\!+\!\lambda\left\langle\varphi_{R},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g_{T,x}^{2}\mathrm{d}x\,\varphi_{R}\right\rangle\!+\!\lambda^{-1}\frac{4\pi(R\!+\!r)^{3}}{3}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}\!+\!\alpha^{-2}\right)\!\!.

Using the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G_{n,m}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g_{T}(y)\mathrm{d}y\delta_{n,m}=\delta_{n,m}
, we obtain for a suitable constant
C>0
and all
R\geq 1
the operator inequality

\displaystyle\|g\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho}_{R})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq C\left(\left(1+\lambda^{-1}R^{3}\right)\!\!\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}+\lambda T^{3}+\alpha^{-2}R^{3}\right),

where we have used
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g_{T,x}^{2}\mathrm{d}x=T^{3}\|g\|^{2}
. Furthermore, note that for a suitable
C>0

\displaystyle\|g\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}\!-\!\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle\leq\|g\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho}_{R})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\|g\!*\!(\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho}_{R})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\|g\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}\!-\!\widetilde{\rho}_{R})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\frac{C}{R}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}^{*}b_{n}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a(u_{n})-\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{n}\right)^{*}\left(a(u_{n})-\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{n}\right)+2\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}-\varphi_{R}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a(u_{n})-\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{n}\right)^{*}\left(a(u_{n})-\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{n}\right)+\frac{C}{R},

see Lemma
2.1
, and

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a(u_{n})-\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{n}\right)^{*}\left(a(u_{n})-\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{n}\right)=W^{-1}_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\mathcal{N}W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}},

where
W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}
is the Weyl operator defined in Eq. (
2.10
). Consequently

\displaystyle\ \ \sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3n}}\|g\!*\!(\rho_{Y_{n}}-\rho^{\mathrm{Pek}})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\!\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\Phi_{\alpha}(x;Y_{n})|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)\!\mathrm{d}Y_{n}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\left(1+\lambda^{-1}R^{3}\right)\!\!\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},W^{-1}_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\mathcal{N}W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle+2C\left(\lambda T^{3}+\alpha^{-2}R^{3}+R^{-1}\right).

By our choice of
\eta
and Lemma
9.2
there exist
\kappa,C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},W^{-1}_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\mathcal{N}W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle\leq C\alpha^{-\kappa}.

Optimizing in
\lambda
and
R
concludes the proof.
∎

Finally, let us show in the following Lemma
9.4
that the state
\Phi_{\alpha}
is close to a tensor state of the form
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\otimes\Phi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
for some
\Phi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Recall the definition of
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
and
Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}
from Subsection
2.3
, and let

\displaystyle Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}:=Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}.

Furthermore let
\Phi_{\alpha}
be the state defined in Eq. (
3.27
). Then there exist
C,\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\|Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha}\|\leq C\alpha^{-\epsilon}.

Proof.

Combining Eq. (
9.1
), for
K:=\alpha
, and the trivial observation that
E_{\alpha}\leq e^{\mathrm{Pek}}
yields

(9.9)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},-\Delta_{x}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}+\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}-2\mathfrak{Re}\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},a(v^{K}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathbb{H}^{K}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq e^{\mathrm{Pek}}+\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}.

In order to analyse the term
\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},a(v^{K}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle
, let us define the operators

\displaystyle b_{j}:

\displaystyle=a(u_{n})-\left\langle u_{n},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}b_{j}^{*}b_{j}=W^{-1}_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\mathcal{N}W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}},

where
\{u_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
is an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
and
W_{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}
is the Weyl transformation introduced in Eq. (
2.10
). With this at hand we can write

(9.10)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},a(v^{K}_{x})\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\!=\!\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left\langle v^{K}_{x},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\!+\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left\langle v^{K}_{x},u_{j}\right\rangle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},b_{j}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}.

Since
\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}=\left|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}*v
and
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\in H^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
, see
[
13
,
21
]
, we have
\left\||k|^{2}\widehat{\left|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}}(k)\right\|_{\infty}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|\left\langle v_{x},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle\!-\!\left\langle v^{K}_{x},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle\right|\!\lesssim\!\int_{|k|>K}\left|\widehat{\left|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right|^{2}}(k)\right||\widehat{v}(k)|^{2}\mathrm{d}k\!\lesssim\!\int_{|k|>K}|k|^{-4}\mathrm{d}k\!\lesssim\!\frac{1}{K}\!=\!\alpha^{-1}.

Defining the multiplication operator
V(x):=-2\mathfrak{Re}\left\langle v_{x},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle
acting on
L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
, there consequently exists a constant
C>0
such that

(9.11)

\displaystyle-2\mathfrak{Re}\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\left\langle v^{K}_{x},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\rangle\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},V\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle-\frac{C}{\alpha}.

Regarding the second term in Eq. (
9.10
), let us define for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
the elements

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}^{K}_{x}:=\frac{\chi(1\leq|\nabla|\leq K)}{i\Delta}\nabla v_{x}

and compute in analogy to Lemma
2.1

\displaystyle v^{K}_{x}=v^{1}_{x}+\left[\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x},\widetilde{w}^{K}_{x}\right],

see also
[
18
]
. Following the approach in
[
4
, Section 2]
we have for
\lambda>0
the estimates

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left\langle v_{x}^{1},u_{j}\right\rangle b_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\|v^{1}\|^{2}+\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{1}{i}\nabla_{x},\left\langle\widetilde{w}^{K}_{x},u_{j}\right\rangle b_{j}\right]

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda\Delta_{x}+\lambda^{-1}\|\widetilde{w}^{K}\|^{2}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}+\alpha^{-2}\right).

Using that
\|v^{1}\|\lesssim 1
and
\|\widetilde{w}^{K}\|^{2}\leq\|w^{K}\|^{2}\lesssim 1
, see Lemma
2.1
, as well as
\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle\leq C\alpha^{-\kappa}
, see Lemma
9.2
, and

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\!-\Delta_{x}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\!L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\!\leq\!\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\!(\mathbb{H}^{K}\!+\!C)\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\!L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\!\leq\!e^{\mathrm{Pek}}\!+\!C\!+\!\alpha^{-\frac{4}{29}}\!,

see Eq. (
7.2
) and Eq. (
9.9
), we obtain for suitable
C,\kappa>0

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\mathfrak{Re}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left\langle v^{K}_{x},u_{j}\right\rangle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},b_{j}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\|v^{1}\|^{2}+\lambda^{-1}\alpha^{-2}+\lambda^{-1}\left(1+\|w^{K}\|^{2}\right)\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}+\lambda\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\!-\Delta_{x}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{\!L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}

(9.12)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \geq-C\lambda-C\lambda^{-1}\alpha^{-\kappa}=-2C\alpha^{-\frac{\kappa}{2}},

where we have optimized in
\lambda
in the last identity. In a similar fashion we obtain

(9.13)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},\mathcal{N}\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\geq\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|-C\alpha^{-\frac{\kappa}{2}}.

Combining Eq. (
9.9
), Eq. (
9.10
), Eq. (
9.11
), Eq. (
9.12
) and Eq. (
9.13
), and defining the operator

\displaystyle h:=-\Delta_{x}+V

acting on
L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
, yields for suitable constants
C,\kappa>0

(9.14)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},h\otimes 1_{\mathcal{F}}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq e^{\mathrm{Pek}}-\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+C\alpha^{-\kappa}.

Note that by the variational definition of
e^{\mathrm{Pek}}
in Eq. (
2.14
), it is clear that
e^{\mathrm{Pek}}-\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}<0
is the ground state energy of
h
with the unique ground state
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
, and since the essential spectrum of
h
is given by
[0,\infty)
, there exists a
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\sigma(h)\cap(e^{\mathrm{Pek}}-\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}-\delta,e^{\mathrm{Pek}}-\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\delta)=\{e^{\mathrm{Pek}}-\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}\},

or equivalently

\displaystyle h\geq e^{\mathrm{Pek}}-\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}+\delta Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}.

Together with Eq. (
9.14
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\Phi_{\alpha}\|^{2}=\left\langle\Phi_{\alpha},Q^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\,\Phi_{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\otimes\mathcal{F}}\leq\frac{C}{\delta}\alpha^{-\kappa}.

∎

Appendix A
Properties of the Semi-Classical minimizers

In the following we are going to prove the properties of the semi-classical minimizers
(\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}})
and the truncated interaction terms
v^{\Lambda}
and
w^{\Lambda}
stated in Lemma
2.1
. For the readers convenience, we are going to display the claimed identities and estimates again

(A.1)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ |\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}*v=\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},

(A.2)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ v=v^{\Lambda}+\frac{1}{i}\nabla\cdot w^{\Lambda},

(A.3)

\displaystyle\ \ \left\|v^{\Lambda}\right\|\leq C\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}},\ \ \ \ \left\|w^{\Lambda}\right\|\leq C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(A.4)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left\|\chi\!\left(|\nabla|>\Lambda\right)\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\|\leq C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(A.5)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left\|\chi\!\left(|y|>R\right)\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\|\leq CR^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(A.6)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\partial_{y_{k}}\frac{\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\partial_{y_{\ell}}\partial_{y_{k}}\frac{\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}{\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}}\right\|_{\infty}<\infty.

Proof of Lemma
2.1
.

Eq. (
A.2
) follows immediately from the operator identity

\displaystyle 1=\chi(|\nabla|\leq\Lambda)+\chi(|\nabla|>\Lambda)=\chi(|\nabla|\leq\Lambda)+\left(\frac{1}{i}\nabla\cdot\frac{i\nabla}{\Delta}\right)\chi(|\nabla|>\Lambda),

where we have used
\Delta=\nabla\cdot\nabla
. Furthermore, note that
\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
minimizes the energy functional

\displaystyle\varphi\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{Pek}}(\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\varphi)=\left\|\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\|^{2}-\left\||\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}*v\right\|^{2}+\left\|\varphi-|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}*v\right\|^{2},

which obtains its unique minimum at
\varphi=|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}*v
, hence
\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}=|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}*v
. Regarding the proof of Eq. (
A.3
) note that the Fourier transformation of
v^{\Lambda}
and
w^{\Lambda}
are given by

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{\Lambda}(k)=(2\pi)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi(|k|\leq\Lambda)|k|^{-1},\ \ \ \widehat{w}^{\Lambda}(k)=(2\pi)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi(|k|>\Lambda)|k|^{-3}k.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\left\|v^{\Lambda}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\chi(|k|\leq\Lambda)|k|^{-2}\mathrm{d}k=2\Lambda,

\displaystyle\left\|w^{\Lambda}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\chi(|k|>\Lambda)|k|^{-4}\mathrm{d}k=2\Lambda^{-1}.

When it comes to Eq. (
A.4
), we compute

\displaystyle\ \left\|\chi\!\left(|\nabla|>\Lambda\right)\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right\|=\left\|\chi\!\left(|\nabla|>\Lambda\right)|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}*v\right\|=\left\||\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}*\left(\nabla\cdot w^{\Lambda}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\nabla|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}\right)*w^{\Lambda}\right\|\leq\|\nabla|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)}\|w^{\Lambda}\|\leq\|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|\|\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|\|w^{\Lambda}\|.

Since
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\in H^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
and
\|w^{\Lambda}\|\leq C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}
by Eq. (
A.3
), this concludes the proof of Eq. (
A.4
). Using Eq. (
A.1
) we clearly have

(A.7)

\displaystyle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(x)=\pi^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)^{2}}{|x-y|^{2}}\mathrm{d}y\geq\pi^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\chi(|y|\leq 1)\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)^{2}\mathrm{d}y\frac{1}{(|x|+1)^{2}}.

By
[
13
]
we know that
\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}>0
, hence Eq. (
A.7
) implies that for a suitable
c>0

\displaystyle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)\geq\frac{c}{(1+|y|)^{2}}.

Consequently, both Eq. (
A.5
) and Eq. (
A.6
) follows once we have established

\displaystyle\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)+|\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)|+|\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)|+|\partial_{y_{\ell}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{y_{j}}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|y|)^{2}}

for a suitable
C>0
. Again by Eq. (
A.1
), it is enough to verify that the functions

(A.8)

\displaystyle\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y),\partial_{y_{j}}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y),\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{y_{j}}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y),\partial_{y_{\ell}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{y_{j}}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)

are bounded from above by
Ce^{-\gamma|y|}
for suitable constants
\gamma,C>0
. In the case of
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
this has been verified in
[
21
]
, and furthermore we know by
[
13
]
that
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Since the function
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
is radial, i.e.

(A.9)

\displaystyle\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)=\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}(|y|),

the exponential decay of the functions in Eq. (
A.8
) follows from the exponential decay of

\displaystyle\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\Delta\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}},\Delta\left(\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right).

In order to verify the exponential decay of
\Delta\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
, note that
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
as the ground state of the operator
-\Delta_{x}-2v*\mathfrak{Re}\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
satisfies the equation

(A.10)

\displaystyle\Delta\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}=2\left(v*\left(v*|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}\right)+\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}-e^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}.

Since we already know that
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
decays exponentially, it follows that
v*(v*|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2})
is bounded and therefore the right hand side of Eq. (
A.10
) decays exponentially. Equivalently, we obtain that the function
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}
in Eq. (
A.9
) satisfies for suitable
C,\gamma>0

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}\left(r^{2}\partial_{r}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}\right)\right|\leq Ce^{-\gamma r}.

Since
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}
decays exponentially as well, we obtain by interpolation that

\displaystyle|\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}(y)|=|\partial_{r}\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}_{*}(|y|)|

satisfies an exponential decay. Taking the gradient of Eq. (
A.10
) furthermore yields

\displaystyle\Delta\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}=2\left(v*\left(v*|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}\right)+\|\varphi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\|^{2}-e^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}+4\left(v*\left(v*\left(\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\right)\right)\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}.

Since
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
decays exponentially it is clear that
v*\left(v*\left(\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}\right)\right)
is bounded. Furthermore, we have already seen that
v*\left(v*|\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}|^{2}\right)
is bounded and that
\nabla\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
as well as
\psi^{\mathrm{Pek}}
decay exponentially, which concludes the proof.
∎
